in terms of modified local drift functions

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{x}}(p),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},&r_{\mathsf{x}}(p):=\max\{|y|,|z|\},\\
V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p)),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},&r_{\mathsf{y}}(p):=\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}},\\
V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{z}}(p),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},&r_{\mathsf{z}}(p):=\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}},\end{array}

for
p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, and some
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
fixed. We show that each
V_{\sigma,\kappa}
satisfies a Lyapunov-drift condition for the stochastic flow map
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
, for
\sigma\in\Sigma
. To do so, we first show in Lemma
10.3
,
10.16
and
10.29
that on a compact set
\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0})^{c}
away from
F_{\sigma}
the drift function
V_{\sigma,\kappa}
is bounded in expectation, for
\kappa>0
small enough. For points
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0})
we next distinguish three regions
\mathcal{K}_{A_{0}}
,
\mathcal{K}_{A_{1}}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{A_{0}}
and
\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{A_{1}}
as portrayed in Figure
6
, for some
A_{1}>A_{0}>1
to be determined, where for
A>1
we denote

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{A}=\{p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}:r_{\sigma}(p)\leq\sqrt{\kappa}A\}.

We call
\mathcal{K}_{A_{1}}
the stochastic regime, where the effects of the Brownian motion are of the same order as those of the velocity field. In this region, we further distinguish the inner core
\mathcal{K}_{A_{0}}
, where the stochasticity of the Brownian motion is enough to move the process quantitatively away from the fixed point with sufficiently high probability, and the annular region
\mathcal{K}_{A_{1}}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{A_{0}}
, where the shearing mechanism is still sufficiently strong to produce a drift estimate for
\mathrm{V}_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}
. Finally,
\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{A_{1}}
denotes the inviscid dominated regime, where the stochastic evolution can be understood as a small perturbation of the deterministic dynamics and the arguments of Lemmas
5.7
,
5.8
and
5.9
can be applied.

Figure 6
.
Stochastic dynamics near fixed points
F_{\sigma}
. On the left, the configuration after one iteration of
10^{5}
tracers on
{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, shown from the south pole, with
\omega_{1}\approx 0.91
,
\omega_{2}\approx-0.47
and
\kappa=5\times 10^{-4}
.
On the right, the corresponding three-zone decomposition around the fixed point.

Recall from Section
1
that, for
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in[-N,N]^{2}
,
t\in[0,2]
and
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, the associated Rossby-Haurwitz vector field is

(10.5)

\displaystyle u_{\omega}(t,p):=\omega_{1}u^{3}(p)\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)}(t)+\omega_{2}u^{2}(p)\mathbf{1}_{[1,2)}(t).

Moreover,
\phi_{t}^{\kappa}
satisfies

(10.6)

\left\{\begin{aligned} {\rm d}\phi_{t}^{\kappa}&=\bigl(u_{\omega}(t,\phi_{t}^{\kappa})-2\kappa\phi_{t}^{\kappa}\bigr)\,{\rm d}t+\sqrt{2\kappa}\,\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\,{\rm d}{\mathbf{B}}_{t},\\
\phi_{0}^{\kappa}&=p,\end{aligned}\right.

where
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, while
\phi_{t}
solves the corresponding
\kappa=0
deterministic equation,

(10.7)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \dot{\phi}_{t}&=u_{\omega}(t,\phi_{t}),\\
\phi_{0}&=p.\end{aligned}\right.

Finally, recall also that

(10.8)

\displaystyle f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p):=\phi_{2}^{\kappa},\qquad f_{\omega}(p):=\phi_{2}.

Before specializing to each set of fixed points
F_{\sigma}
, we begin by applying Lemma
9.6
to record the stability in expectation of
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
with respect to
f_{\omega}
.

Lemma 10.1
.

There exists
C_{N}=C(N)>0
such that

(10.9)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}|\phi^{\kappa}_{1}-\phi_{1}|^{2}\leq C_{N}\kappa,\qquad{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|^{2}\leq C_{N}\kappa.

for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
, all
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, and all
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in[-N,N]^{2}
,

Proof.

The first inequality follows directly from Lemma
9.1
with
u=\omega_{1}u^{3}
and
t=1
, after noticing that
C_{u}
is uniform in
\omega_{1}\in[-N,N]
since
|\omega_{1}|\leq N
. Hence, for some
C_{1,N}>0
,

(10.10)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}|\phi_{1}^{\kappa}-\phi_{1}|^{2}\leq C_{1,N}\kappa.

For the second inequality, we introduce an intermediate point
\widehat{\phi}_{2}:=f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}(\phi_{1}^{\kappa})
, which represent the evolution under the deterministic flow
f^{2}_{\omega_{2}}
of the random endpoint
\phi_{1}^{\kappa}
.
Then

(10.11)

|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|^{2}\leq 2|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\widehat{\phi}_{2}|^{2}+2|\widehat{\phi}_{2}-\phi_{2}|^{2},

where conditioning on
\phi_{1}^{\kappa}
and using Lemma
9.1
with
u=\omega_{2}u^{2}
and
t=1
leads to the bound

(10.12)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\left[\,|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\widehat{\phi}_{2}|^{2}\,\big|\,\phi_{1}^{\kappa}\right]\leq C_{1,N}\kappa,

thus
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\widehat{\phi}_{2}|^{2}\leq C_{1,N}\kappa
.

For the second term, since
f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}
is a smooth diffeomorphism of
{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
and
\omega_{2}\in[-N,N]
, there exists
C_{2,N}>0
such that

(10.13)

|\widehat{\phi}_{2}-\phi_{2}|=|f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}(\phi_{1}^{\kappa})-f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}(\phi_{1})|\leq C_{2,N}|\phi_{1}^{\kappa}-\phi_{1}|.

As a result,
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}|\widehat{\phi}_{2}-\phi_{2}|^{2}\leq C_{1,N}C_{2,N}^{2}\kappa
and combining the two estimates proves the lemma.
∎

10.1.
Stochastic drift function for
F_{\mathsf{x}}
fixed points

We now turn our attention to the fixed points present in the inviscid dynamics.
Starting from
F_{\mathsf{x}}=\{(\pm 1,0,0)\}
, we discuss the drift condition for the viscous Lyapunov function

(10.14)

\displaystyle V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{x}}(p),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},\qquad r_{\mathsf{x}}(p):=\max\{|y|,|z|\},

where
p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, and throughout we fix
\alpha\in\Bigl(0,\frac{1}{4}\Bigr)
.

We work around the fixed point
(1,0,0)
, noting that all arguments apply to its opposite pole by symmetry. We define, for
y^{2}+z^{2}<s_{0}^{2}
, the local chart and its corresponding neighbourhood respectively

(10.15)

\displaystyle\Psi_{{\mathsf{x}}}(y,z):=\bigl(\sqrt{1-y^{2}-z^{2}},\,y,\,z\bigr),\qquad\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{x}}}(s_{0}):=\Psi_{{\mathsf{x}}}\bigl(\{(y,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:\ y^{2}+z^{2}<s_{0}^{2}\}\bigr).

The goal of this subsection is the following Lyapunov-Foster drift estimate.

Proposition 10.2
.

There exists
s_{0}
small enough such that there exist
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta_{\mathsf{x}}\geq 1
and
\kappa_{0}>0
for which

(10.16)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma\,V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)+\beta_{\mathsf{x}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p)

for all
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
and for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

As we previously commented, the proof of the proposition is divided according to the distance of
p
to the fixed points
F_{\mathsf{x}}
, given by
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)
, relative to the stochastic noise, which is of order
\sqrt{\kappa}
.

From Lemma
10.1
it follows immediately a uniform bound in
\kappa\in(0,1)
for the expected value of
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})
for points outside the small cap
\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{x}}}(s_{0})^{c}
.

Lemma 10.3
.

There exists
C>0
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.17)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})]\leq C

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{x}}}(s_{0})^{c}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

Since the mapping
(p,\omega)\mapsto r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2}(p,\omega))
is continuous on the compact set
\mathcal{K}:=\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})^{c}\times[-N,N]^{2}
, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that
r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2})\geq c_{0}
for all points in
\mathcal{K}
.
Now, using that the map
r_{\mathsf{x}}
is Lipschitz continuous on
{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
with constant
1
, we define

(10.18)

G:=\{|\phi^{\kappa}_{2}-\phi_{2}|\leq\delta_{0}\},\qquad\delta_{0}:=\frac{c_{0}}{2}.

On
G
we use Lipschitz continuity to write

(10.19)

V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\leq r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})^{-\alpha}\leq(r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2})-|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|)^{-\alpha}\leq\left(\frac{c_{0}}{2}\right)^{-\alpha},

while on
G^{c}
, using Chebyshev’s inequality and Lemma
10.1
, we estimate

(10.20)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}(G^{c})\leq\delta_{0}^{-2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|^{2}\leq C_{N}\delta_{0}^{-2}\kappa.

Finally, since on
G^{c}
holds the trivial bound
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\leq\kappa^{-\alpha/2}
, we deduce

(10.21)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})|G]{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}(G)+{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})|G^{c}]{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}(G^{c})\leq\left(\frac{c_{0}}{2}\right)^{-\alpha}+C_{N}\delta_{0}^{-2}\kappa^{1-\alpha/2},

which, since
\alpha<2
, is uniform in
\kappa\in(0,1)
.
∎

10.1.1.
Stochastic regime

We consider here those
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
such that
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
, for some
A>1
to be determined. The point
p
is now at distance of order
\sqrt{\kappa}
to the fixed point
(1,0,0)
and since the velocity field vanishes linearly at
(1,0,0)
we observe that diffusion and advection are of the same order. To precisely describe the motion in this regime, we rescale our variables.

For
A>1
we define the compact set

(10.22)

\displaystyle K_{A}:=\{(y,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:\ \max\{|y|,|z|\}\leq A\},

and, for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the functions

(10.23)

\displaystyle g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z):=h(y,z)^{-\alpha},\quad h(y,z):=\max\{|y|,|z|,1\}.

We have the trivial bounds
A^{-\alpha}\leq g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)\leq 1
, for all
(y,z)\in K_{A}
and for
p=\Psi_{\mathsf{x}}(\sqrt{\kappa}y,\sqrt{\kappa}z)\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
, we have

(10.24)

\displaystyle V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)=\kappa^{-\alpha/2}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z).

Starting from
u_{\omega}
defined in (
10.5
), we introduce the rescaled two-step process

(10.25)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\left((f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(\Psi_{\mathsf{x}}(\sqrt{\kappa}y,\sqrt{\kappa}z)))_{y},(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(\Psi_{\mathsf{x}}(\sqrt{\kappa}y,\sqrt{\kappa}z)))_{z}\right),

which can be rewritten as

(10.26)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z):=\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}((\phi_{2}^{\kappa})_{y},(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})_{z}),

where
\phi_{2}^{\kappa}
is the solution at time
2
of the stochastic differential equation (
10.6
) with initial datum
\phi_{0}^{\kappa}=\Psi_{\mathsf{x}}(\sqrt{\kappa}y,\sqrt{\kappa}z)
.

For
(y,z)\in K_{A}
we have the following equivalence result.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
A>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.27)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
with
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
,

(2)

There exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.28)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z))]\leq\gamma g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z),

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
(y,z)\in K_{A}
,

Proof.

For
p=\Psi_{\mathsf{x}}(\sqrt{\kappa}y,\sqrt{\kappa}z)
, we have
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
if and only if
(y,z)\in K_{A}
. By definition of
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z)
, we have

(10.29)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{-\alpha/2}{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z))]

for all
(y,z)\in K_{A}
. In view of (
10.24
), the lemma is proved.
∎

Thanks to the above lemma, we next aim to show
g_{\mathsf{x}}
satisfies the drift condition (
10.28
) for
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}
. To that purpose, we first obtain the exact stochastic differential equations satisfied by the rescaled one-point process.
We denote it as
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}:=(\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa},\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa})
for consistency, hence having
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}=\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{\kappa}
.
Using the explicit formulas

(10.30)

\displaystyle u^{3}(x,y,z)=z(-y,x,0),\qquad u^{2}(x,y,z)=y(z,0,-x).

we obtain for
t\in[0,1)
and
t\in[1,2)
respectively

(10.31)

\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}&=\left(\omega_{1}x_{t}^{\kappa}\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa}-2\kappa\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{2}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{B}}_{t},\\
d\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa}&=-2\kappa\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{3}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{B}}_{t},\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}&=-2\kappa\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{2}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t},\\
d\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa}&=-\left(\omega_{2}x_{t}^{\kappa}\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}+2\kappa\widetilde{z}_{t}^{\kappa}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{3}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t}={\mathbf{B}}_{t}-{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
,
x_{t}^{\kappa}=(\phi_{t}^{\kappa})_{x}=\sqrt{1-(y_{t}^{\kappa})^{2}-(z_{t}^{\kappa})^{2}}
for
t\in[1,2)
, and

(10.32)

\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}=\begin{pmatrix}1-(x_{t}^{\kappa})^{2}&-x_{t}^{\kappa}y_{t}^{\kappa}&-x_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}\\
-x_{t}^{\kappa}y_{t}^{\kappa}&1-(y_{t}^{\kappa})^{2}&-y_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}\\
-x_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}&-y_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}&1-(z_{t}^{\kappa})^{2}\end{pmatrix}.

The aim is to compare
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}
with the corresponding limiting solution
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}:=(\widetilde{y}_{t},\widetilde{z}_{t})
at time
t=2
(i.e.
\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}
and
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z)
respectively), which satisfies, for
t\in[0,1)
and
t\in[1,2)
respectively,

(10.33)

\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{y}_{t}&=\omega_{1}\widetilde{z}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{2},\\
d\widetilde{z}_{t}&=\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{3},\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{y}_{t}&=\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{B}_{t}^{2},\\
d\widetilde{z}_{t}&=-\omega_{2}\widetilde{y}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{B}_{t}^{3}.\end{aligned}\right.

The comparison between
\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}
is perturbative only as long as the rescaled state remains moderate in size.
This motivates the introduction of the following stopping time
4
4

4

The exponent
\frac{1}{4}
is chosen to balance perturbative remainders in (
10.31
) with the exit event contribution.

(10.34)

\displaystyle\tau_{\kappa}:=\inf\bigl\{t\in[0,2):\ |\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}|\geq\kappa^{-\frac{1}{4}}\bigr\}\wedge 2.

Indeed, if
|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}|\leq\kappa^{-\frac{1}{4}}
, for
\kappa
small enough

(10.35)

\displaystyle x_{t}^{\kappa}=\sqrt{1-\kappa|(\widetilde{y}^{\kappa}_{t},\widetilde{z}^{\kappa}_{t})|^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

namely the path
t\mapsto\phi_{t}^{\kappa}
starting from
(y,z)\in K_{A}
remains in the chart around
(1,0,0)
.

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
A,N>1
. There exists a constant
C_{A,N}>0
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
and all
(y,z)\in K_{A}
there holds

(10.36)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}(y,z)-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z)|\bigr]\leq C_{A,N}\kappa^{1/4},

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
, where
\kappa_{0}>0
is chosen small enough so that (
10.35
) applies. Let
\tau_{\kappa}
be the stopping time introduced in (
10.34
) and decompose

(10.37)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[|\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}|\Bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[|\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}|\Big|\tau_{\kappa}\geq 2\Bigr]{\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{\kappa}\geq 2)+{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[|\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}|\big|\tau_{\kappa}<2\Bigr]{\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{\kappa}<2)

(10.38)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{2}-\widetilde{\phi}_{2}|\Bigr]+{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{2}|+\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}_{2}|\right)1_{\{\tau_{\kappa}<2\}}\Bigr]

(10.39)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}-\widetilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]^{1/2}+\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr){\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{\kappa}<2)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

A standard Burkholder-Davis-Gundy argument and Grönwall inequality applied to the processes
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}
,
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}
, and
\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}-\widetilde{\phi}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}
yields

(10.40)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C_{A,N},\qquad{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C_{A,N},\qquad{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}-\widetilde{\phi}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C_{A,N}\kappa^{1/2},

where
\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}
satisfy the stopped version of (
10.31
) and (
10.33
) respectively.
Indeed, with the choice of
\tau_{\kappa}
all the remainder terms are of order
\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}
while
u_{\omega}
is globally Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant bounded by
N
uniformly in
t\in[0,2]
.

We estimate

(10.41)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{\kappa}<2)={\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}|\geq\kappa^{-1/4}\Bigr)\leq\kappa^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2}|\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C_{A,N}\kappa^{1/2},

which combined with (
10.40
) concludes the proof.
∎

It follows immediately from the above proposition that, for small
\kappa>0
, establishing a drift condition for
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
is enough to derive a drift for
\widetilde{f}^{\kappa}_{\omega}
.

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
\alpha,\gamma\in(0,1)
and
A,N>1
. Then there exists
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\alpha,\gamma,A,N)>0
such that

(10.42)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z))-g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))|\bigr]\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)

for all
(y,z)\in K_{A}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

The proposition follows directly from combining
g_{\mathsf{x}}
being globally Lipschitz on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with Lemma
10.5
and the trivial bound
g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)\geq A^{-\alpha}
on
K_{A}
to obtain

(10.43)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(y,z))-g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))|\bigr]\leq C_{\alpha,A,N}A^{\alpha}\kappa^{1/4}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z).

Choosing now
\kappa_{0}>0
such that
C_{\alpha,A,N}A^{\alpha}\kappa_{0}^{1/4}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}
concludes the proof.
∎

Having established that it is enough to obtain a drift estimate for the limiting rescaled process
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
, we integrate explicitly (
10.33
) to write, for
(y,z)\in K_{A}
,

(10.44)

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{y}_{1},\widetilde{z}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(y+\omega_{1}z+\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{2}+\omega_{1}\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}B_{s}^{3}\,{\rm d}s,\quad z+\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{3}\right),

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{2},\quad\widetilde{z}_{1}-\omega_{2}\widetilde{y}_{1}-\omega_{2}\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{3}\right)

where
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in[-N,N]
uniformly and
B_{t}=(B_{t}^{2},B^{3}_{t})
and
\overline{B}_{t}=(\overline{B}_{t}^{2},\overline{B}_{t}^{3})
are independent two-dimensional Brownian motions, and all these objects are mutually independent.
To prove a drift estimate for
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
, we divide the stochastic region
K_{A}=K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}}
into an inner core
K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
and an annular core
K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}}\setminus K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
, for suitably chosen
1<A_{0}<A_{1}
.

10.1.2.
The annular core
K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}}\setminus K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}

Consider here
1<A_{0}<A_{1}
to be determined. The following abstract lemma records stretching estimates of shearing motion with random amplitudes.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exists
C_{\alpha}>0
such that for all
a,b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and all
N\geq 1
,

(10.45)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|a+\omega b|)^{-\alpha}\}\Bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}\min\bigl\{1,(N|a|)^{-\alpha},(N^{2}|b|)^{-\alpha}\bigr\},

where
\omega\sim\mathrm{Unif}([-N,N])
.

Proof.

Set

(10.46)

I(a,b,N):=\frac{1}{2N}\int_{-N}^{N}\min\{1,(N|a+\omega b|)^{-\alpha}\}\,{\rm d}\omega

and note that for
a=b=0
the estimate is trivial. We split the proof into two cases based on the sizes of
N|b|
and
|a|
.

\diamond

Case 1.
Assume
2N|b|\leq|a|
and note that for every
\omega\in[-N,N]
, it holds
|a+\omega b|\geq\frac{|a|}{2}
so that

(10.47)

I(a,b,N)\leq\frac{1}{2N}\int_{-N}^{N}\min\{1,(N|a+\omega b|)^{-\alpha}\}\,{\rm d}\omega\leq\min\{1,(N|a|/2)^{-\alpha}\}\leq 2^{\alpha}\min\{1,(N|a|)^{-\alpha}\}.

\diamond

Case 2.
Consider now
2N|b|>|a|
, which in particular implies
b\neq 0
. Since the function

(10.48)

u\longmapsto\min\{1,(N|u|)^{-\alpha}\}

is even and non-increasing on
[0,\infty)
, among all intervals of fixed length
2N|b|
the integral is maximised by the interval centred at the origin. Therefore

(10.49)

\displaystyle I(a,b,N)\leq\frac{1}{2N|b|}\int_{-N|b|}^{N|b|}\min\{1,(N|u|)^{-\alpha}\}\,{\rm d}u=\frac{1}{2N^{2}|b|}\int_{-N^{2}|b|}^{N^{2}|b|}\min\{1,|v|^{-\alpha}\}\,{\rm d}v,

upon changing variables
v=Nu
.
Computing the last integral explicitly leads to
I(a,b,N)\leq 1
when
N^{2}|b|\leq 1
and
I(a,b,N)\leq C_{\alpha}(N^{2}|b|)^{-\alpha}
when
N^{2}|b|\geq 1
, hence

(10.50)

I(a,b,N)\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(N^{2}|b|)^{-\alpha}\}.

Combining the two cases concludes the proof.
∎

We immediately apply this lemma to determine the threshold
A_{0}>1
such that
g_{\mathsf{x}}
satisfies a local Lyapunov-drift condition for the limiting rescaled dynamics in the annular region
(K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}})^{c}
.

Proposition 10.8
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
A_{0}=A_{0}(\alpha)>1
and
N_{\infty}=N_{\infty}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
N\geq N_{\infty}
and every
(y,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with
h(y,z)\geq A_{0}
, there holds

(10.51)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))]\leq\frac{1}{2}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z).

Proof.

Fix
(y,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and let
h_{0}:=h(y,z)\geq 1
. Let
\widetilde{f}_{\omega_{1}}(y,z)=(\widetilde{y}_{1},\widetilde{z}_{1})
and
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z)=(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2})
be given by (
10.44
). Since
g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2})\leq\min\{1,|\widetilde{z}_{2}|^{-\alpha}\}
, it suffices to estimate
|\widetilde{z}_{2}|
. Set

(10.52)

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}:=\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s,\quad\Theta_{2}:=\widetilde{z}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{3}.

Then
\widetilde{z}_{2}=\Theta_{2}-\omega_{2}\Theta_{1}
and we obtain

(10.53)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{2}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{2}}[\min\{1,|\Theta_{2}-\omega_{2}\Theta_{1}|^{-\alpha}\}]\leq C_{1,\alpha}\,{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\Bigr],

where the last inequality follows from the same argument used in the proof of Lemma
10.7
.
We next note that
\Theta_{1}=\Lambda_{1}+\omega_{1}\Lambda_{2}
, for

(10.54)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}:=y+\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{2}+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s,\quad\Lambda_{2}:=z+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}B_{s}^{3}\,{\rm d}s.

Setting
G_{1}:=\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{2}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,2)
,
G_{2}:=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,2/3)
and
G_{3}:=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}B_{s}^{3}\,{\rm d}s\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,2/3)
, we consider

(10.55)

\displaystyle F:=\left\{|G_{1}|+|G_{2}|+|G_{3}|\leq\frac{h_{0}}{4}\right\}.

On
F
we have
|\Lambda_{1}-y|\leq\frac{h_{0}}{4}
and also
|\Lambda_{2}-z|\leq\frac{h_{0}}{4}
and therefore
\max\{|\Lambda_{1}|,|\Lambda_{2}|\}\geq\frac{3h_{0}}{4}
. Letting
A_{0}>1
we have
h_{0}\geq 1
and for
N>1
we see that Lemma
10.7
implies the existence of
C_{2,\alpha}>0
, depending only on
\alpha
, such that

(10.56)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},{\mathbf{B}}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}|F\Bigr]={\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Lambda_{1}+\omega_{1}\Lambda_{2}|)^{-\alpha}\}|F\bigr]\Bigr]\leq C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}.

On the other hand,

(10.57)

\displaystyle F^{c}\subset\Bigl\{|G_{1}|>\frac{h_{0}}{12}\Bigr\}\cup\Bigl\{|G_{2}|>\frac{h_{0}}{12}\Bigr\}\cup\Bigl\{|G_{3}|>\frac{h_{0}}{12}\Bigr\}

and since each
G_{j}
is a Gaussian random variable, there exist universal constants
c,C>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})\leq Ce^{-ch_{0}^{2}}
. In particular, we may choose
A_{0}>1
large enough depending only on
c
,
C
,
C_{1,\alpha}
so that
C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})\leq\frac{1}{4}h_{0}^{-\alpha}
for all
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
. Summing up, for
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
, we deduce that

(10.58)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},{\mathbf{B}}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}|F\Bigr]+C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},{\mathbf{B}}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}|F^{c}\Bigr]{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})

(10.59)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}+C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})

(10.60)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}+\frac{1}{4}h_{0}^{-\alpha}.

Choosing
N_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}
so large that
C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for all
N\geq N_{\infty}
, the lemma is proved.
∎

10.1.3.
The inner core
K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}

Once
A_{0}
is determined by Proposition
10.8
above, we now turn to studying the dynamics in the core
K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
. Before proving a drift estimate for
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
in this region, we first need some preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 10.9
.

Let
\zeta
be a real-valued random variable with probability density function
\rho:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
satisfying
\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq M
. Then, for every
c\in(0,1]
,

(10.61)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\min\left\{1,\frac{c}{|\zeta|}\right\}\right]\leq\left(1+2M+2M\log(c^{-1})\right)c.

Proof.

Splitting the expectation into the three regions
\{|\zeta|\leq c\}
,
\{c<|\zeta|\leq 1\}
, and
\{|\zeta|>1\}
we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\min\left\{1,\frac{c}{|\zeta|}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|\leq c)+c\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\zeta|^{-1}\big|c<|\zeta|\leq 1\right]{\mathbb{P}}(c<|\zeta|\leq 1)+c\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\zeta|^{-1}\big||\zeta|>1\right]{\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|>1)

(10.62)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|\leq c)+c\int_{\{c<|x|\leq 1\}}\frac{\rho(x)}{|x|}\,{\rm d}x+c\,{\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|>1).

Since
\rho\leq M
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|\leq c)\leq 2Mc
, while

(10.63)

\displaystyle\int_{\{c<|x|\leq 1\}}\frac{\rho(x)}{|x|}\,{\rm d}x\leq M\int_{\{c<|x|\leq 1\}}\frac{1}{|x|}\,{\rm d}x=2M\int_{c}^{1}\frac{1}{x}\,{\rm d}x=2M\log(c^{-1}).

Finally,
c\,{\mathbb{P}}(|\zeta|>1)\leq c
and the combination of these estimates completes the proof.
∎

The next lemma shows that the shearing motion localises points with small probability.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
\omega\sim\mathrm{Unif}[-N,N]
with
N\geq 1
, and let
\eta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\upsilon^{2})
be independent of
\omega
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be a
\sigma
-algebra independent of
(\omega,\eta)
, and let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
be real-valued,
\mathcal{H}
-measurable random variables. Then, for every
r\in(0,N]
,

(10.64)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}\right)\leq\frac{2r}{N}\left(1+\upsilon^{-1}+\upsilon^{-1}\log\frac{N}{r}\right)\qquad\text{a.s.,}

In particular,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\right)\leq\frac{2r}{N}\left(1+\upsilon^{-1}+\upsilon^{-1}\log\frac{N}{r}\right)
.

Proof.

Note that the set
a\in[-N,N]
for which
|\Lambda_{1}+a(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r
has Lebesgue measure at most
\min\left\{2N,\frac{2r}{|\Lambda_{2}+\eta|}\right\}
. Since
\omega\sim\text{Unif}[-N,N]
, we have

(10.65)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\,\big|\,\mathcal{H},\eta\right)\leq\min\left\{1,\frac{r}{N|\Lambda_{2}+\eta|}\right\}.

Next,

(10.66)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}\right)={\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}\left[{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\,\big|\,\mathcal{H},\eta\right)\right]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\Lambda_{2}+\eta}\left[\min\left\{1,\frac{r}{N|\Lambda_{2}+\eta|}\right\}\right]

and applying Lemma
10.9
with
c=\frac{r}{N}\in(0,1]
,
\zeta=\Lambda_{2}+\eta\sim\mathcal{N}(\Lambda_{2},\upsilon^{2})
whose probability density function
\varrho_{\Lambda_{2}+\eta}
satisfies
\|\varrho_{\Lambda_{2}+\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq(2\pi\upsilon^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
we obtain

(10.67)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+2(2\pi\upsilon^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2(2\pi\upsilon^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(\frac{N}{r})\right)\frac{r}{N},

as claimed. Finally, set
E=\{|\Lambda_{1}+\omega(\Lambda_{2}+\eta)|\leq r\}
and note that

(10.68)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E)={\mathbb{E}}[1_{E}]={\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbb{E}}[1_{E}|\mathcal{H}]]={\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbb{P}}(E|\mathcal{H})].

The lemma follows as the bound for
{\mathbb{P}}(E|\mathcal{H})
is deterministic and independent of
\mathcal{H}
.
∎

We are now in position to prove a drift for the limiting rescaled process.

Proposition 10.11
.

Let
A_{0}>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. There exists
N_{0}=N_{0}(A_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.69)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))]\leq\gamma g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z),

for all
N\geq N_{0}
and all
(y,z)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
.

Proof.

We begin by recalling that
{A_{0}}^{-\alpha}\leq g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)\leq 1
and
1\leq h(y,z)\leq A_{0}
, for all
(y,z)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
. In particular, there holds

(10.70)

\displaystyle g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(y,z))\leq{A_{0}}^{\alpha}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)

for all
(y,z)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
. Let
(y,z)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
and set
h_{0}=h(y,z)\in[1,A_{0}]
, we define the set
E:=\{|\widetilde{z}_{2}|\leq 2h_{0}\}
. We have

(10.71)

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}_{2}=\left(\widetilde{z}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{3}\right)-\omega_{2}\left(\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s\right).

For the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{H}_{1}:=\sigma(\omega_{1},(B_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\overline{B}_{1}^{3})
we observe that
\widetilde{y}_{1}
and
\widetilde{z}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{3}
are
\mathcal{H}_{1}
-measurable, while
\eta:=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2/3)
is independent of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and of
\omega_{2}
. Using Lemma
10.10
on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
, with

(10.72)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}=\widetilde{z}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{3},\qquad\Lambda_{2}=\widetilde{y}_{1}

and threshold
r=2h_{0}\leq 2A_{0}\leq N
we obtain

(10.73)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E)\leq C\,\frac{2h_{0}}{N}\left(1+\log\frac{N}{2h_{0}}\right)\leq CA_{0}\frac{1+\log N}{N}.

On the other hand, on
E^{c}
we have that
|\widetilde{z}_{2}|\geq 2h_{0}
and
g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2})\leq 2^{-\alpha}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)
. Combining both estimates, we obtain

(10.74)

\displaystyle P_{\mathsf{x}}g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(E){\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2})|E]+{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c}){\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{y}_{2},\widetilde{z}_{2})|E^{c}]

(10.75)

\displaystyle\leq\left(CA_{0}^{\alpha+1}\frac{1+\log(N)}{N}+2^{-\alpha}\right)g_{\mathsf{x}}(y,z)

and the lemma follows, for
\gamma:=\frac{1+2^{-\alpha}}{2}<1
, after choosing
N_{0}=N_{0}(A_{0},\alpha)
such that
CA_{0}^{\alpha+1}\frac{1+\log(N)}{N}\leq\frac{1-2^{-\alpha}}{2}
for all
N\geq N_{0}
large enough.
∎

In view of Propositions
10.6
,
10.8
,
10.11
, and Lemma
10.4
, the following result is immediate.

Corollary 10.12
.

Let
A_{1}>A_{0}
. Then, there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.76)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
satisfying
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\leq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

Lemma
10.4
allows us to work directly on
g_{\mathsf{x}}
, for which

(10.77)

{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{\kappa})]={\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{\kappa})-g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{2})]+{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{x}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{2})].

The second term is
\gamma
-contractive either by Proposition
10.11
, if
(y,z)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
, or
\frac{1}{2}
-contractive by Proposition
10.8
, if
(x,y)\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}}\setminus K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{0}}
.
Applying then Proposition
10.6
with
\bar{\gamma}=\frac{1+\max\{\gamma,\frac{1}{2}\}}{2}
concludes the proof.
∎

10.1.4.
Inviscid-dominated regime

We consider here the region where the inviscid dynamics dominate the stochastic processes by assuming that
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\geq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
, for some
A_{1}\geq A_{0}
to be determined. We show that a version of Lemma
5.7
holds for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
.

Proposition 10.13
.

Let
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
. Then there exist
N_{1}>1
,
A_{1}=A_{1}(N_{1})>A_{0}
,
s_{0}>0
,
\kappa_{0}>0
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that,

(10.78)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p),

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
such that
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\geq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Fix
\eta>0
small, to be chosen during the proof, and set for convenience
r:=r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)=\max\{|y|,|z|\}
and note that
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)=r^{-\alpha}
for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
with
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)\geq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}\geq\sqrt{\kappa}
. We define the set

(10.79)

\displaystyle G:=\left\{|\phi_{1}^{\kappa}-\phi_{1}|\leq\eta r,\ \ |\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|\leq\eta r\right\}

and denote
r_{2}=r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2})
and
r_{1}=r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{1})
. Since
r_{\mathsf{x}}
is
1
-Lipschitz continuous we have
|r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})-r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2})|\leq|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|\leq\eta r
on
G
. We distinguish the two cases as in the deterministic case (see Lemma
5.7
).

\diamond

Case
|y|\leq|z|
.
Here
r=|z|
and
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)=|z|^{-\alpha}
. We recall the splitting
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
, and
E_{3}
for which

–

On
E_{1}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r}{2}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1})\leq\frac{1}{2N^{2}}
.

–

On
E_{2}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r}{4N}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{2})\leq\frac{4}{N}
.

–

On
E_{3}=(E_{1}\cup E_{2})^{c}
there holds
r_{2}\geq 2r
.

Setting
E=E_{3}
, we observe that

(10.80)

\displaystyle r_{\mathsf{x}}(p_{2}^{\kappa})\geq(2-\eta)r\mathbf{1}_{E\cap G}+\left(\frac{1}{4N}-\eta\right)r\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}\cap G}\geq\frac{3r}{2}\mathbf{1}_{E\cap G}+\frac{r}{8N}\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}\cap G}

for
\eta=\frac{1}{8N}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq\frac{8}{N}
for
N
large enough. Thus,

(10.81)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})|G\right]\leq\left(\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{\alpha}+8\frac{(8N)^{\alpha}}{N}\right)V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)

for some
\gamma\in((2/3)^{\alpha},1)
, for
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
and
N_{1}
large enough.

\diamond

Case
|z|\leq|y|
.
Now
r=|y|
and
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)=|y|^{-\alpha}
. We again recall the splitting used in Lemma
5.7
, for which

–

On
E_{1}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r}{8N}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1})\leq\frac{1}{2N^{2}}
.

–

On
E_{2}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r}{8N^{2}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{2})\leq\frac{4}{\sqrt{N}}
.

–

On
E_{3}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r}{\sqrt{N}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{3})\leq\frac{8}{\sqrt{N}}
.

–

On
E_{4}=(E_{1}\cup E_{2}\cup E_{3})^{c}
there holds
r_{2}\geq 2r
.

Hence, setting
E=E_{4}
leads to

(10.82)

\displaystyle r_{\mathsf{x}}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\geq(2-\eta)r\mathbf{1}_{E\cap G}+\left(\frac{1}{8N^{2}}-\eta\right)r\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}\cap G}\geq\frac{3r}{2}\mathbf{1}_{E\cap G}+\frac{r}{16N^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}\cap G}

for
\eta=\frac{1}{16N^{2}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq\frac{16}{\sqrt{N}}
. Therefore,

(10.83)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})|G\right]\leq\left(\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{\alpha}+16\frac{(16N^{2})^{\alpha}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)

for some
\gamma\in((2/3)^{\alpha},1)
, as
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
and choosing
N_{1}
large enough.

Finally, the probability of the bad set
G^{c}
is estimated using Lemma
10.1
and Markov’s inequality as

(10.84)

{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}(G^{c})\leq\frac{C_{N}\sqrt{\kappa}}{\eta r},

which combined with the trivial bound
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}\leq\kappa^{-\alpha/2}
leads to

(10.85)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})|G^{c}\right]{\mathbb{P}}(G^{c})\leq\frac{C_{N}}{\eta}\kappa^{(1-\alpha)/2}r^{-1}=\frac{C_{N}}{\eta}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\kappa}}{r}\right)^{1-\alpha}r^{-\alpha}\leq\frac{C_{N}}{\eta{A_{1}}^{1-\alpha}}V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p).

Choosing
A_{1}=A_{1}(N_{1})>A_{0}
large enough so that
\frac{C_{N}}{\eta A^{1-\alpha}_{1}}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}
concludes the proof.
∎

We can now conclude the local and global Lyapunov estimates.

Proposition 10.14
.

There exist
N>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.86)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

Choose
A_{0}
and
N_{\infty}
from Proposition
10.8
. Then choose
N
so large that

(10.87)

\displaystyle N\geq\max\{N_{\infty},\;N_{0}(A_{0},\alpha),\;N_{\mathrm{out}}\}.

For this
N
, let
A_{1}=A_{1}(N),s_{0}=s_{\mathrm{out}}(N)
, and let
\gamma_{\mathrm{in}},\gamma_{\mathrm{out}}
and
\kappa_{\mathrm{in}},\kappa_{\mathrm{out}}
be given by Proposition
10.13
and Corollary
10.12
respectively. Define

(10.88)

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}:=\min\left\{\kappa_{\mathrm{in}},\kappa_{\mathrm{out}},\frac{s_{0}^{2}}{A_{1}^{2}}\right\}>0,\qquad\gamma:=\max\{\gamma_{\mathrm{in}},\gamma_{\mathrm{out}}\}\in(0,1),

and fix
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
and
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
. Since either
p\in K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}}
or
p\in(K^{\mathsf{x}}_{A_{1}})^{c}
holds, the claim follows from Corollary
10.12
and Proposition
10.13
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
10.2
.

Combining Proposition
10.14
and Lemma
10.3
according to whether
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})
or not yields

(10.89)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)+\beta_{\mathsf{x}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{x}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{X}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.
∎

10.2.
Stochastic drift function for
F_{\mathsf{z}}
fixed points

We adapt the analysis of Section
10.1
to the fixed points
F_{\mathsf{z}}=\{(0,0,\pm 1)\}
.
We define
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
,
f_{\omega}
,
\phi_{1}^{\kappa}
,
\phi_{2}^{\kappa}
,
\phi_{1}
, and
\phi_{2}
exactly as in Section
10.1
, and we further recall that
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p):=\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}
for
p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
. We introduce the diffusive Lyapunov function

(10.90)

V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{z}}(p),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
to be chosen later. Moreover, we define the local chart

(10.91)

\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y):=(x,y,\sqrt{1-x^{2}-y^{2}}),\qquad\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{z}}(s_{0}):=\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,:\,0<x^{2}+y^{2}<s_{0}^{2}\}),

and we remark that by symmetry we will be considering only the point
(0,0,1)
.

The main goal of the section is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 10.15
.

There exists
s_{0}>0
small enough such that there exist
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}\geq 1
, and
\kappa_{0}>0
for which

(10.92)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\!\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma\,V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)+\beta_{\mathsf{z}}\,\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{X}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

In particular, Lemma
10.1
being independent of the fixed points implies that a version of Lemma
10.3
holds near
F_{\mathsf{z}}
after replacing
r_{\mathsf{x}}
with
r_{\mathsf{z}}
in the proof.
We record it here for completeness.

Lemma 10.16
.

There exists
C>0
such that

(10.93)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\right]\leq C,

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})^{c}
and all
\kappa\in(0,1)
.

As in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
case, the proof of Proposition
10.15
is divided according to the size of the distance of
p
to the fixed points
F_{\mathsf{z}}
, measured by
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)
, relative to the stochastic noise, which is of order
\sqrt{\kappa}
.
The key simplification in the present setting is that the first map
f^{3}_{\omega_{1}}
preserves
r_{\mathsf{z}}
exactly.

10.2.1.
Stochastic regime

We consider here those
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
such that
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
, for some
A>1
to be determined. The point
p
is now at distance of order
\sqrt{\kappa}
from the fixed point
(0,0,1)
, and since the velocity field vanishes linearly at
(0,0,1)
, diffusion and advection are of the same order. To precisely describe the motion in this regime, we rescale our variables.

For
A>1
, define the compact set

(10.94)

\displaystyle K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}:=\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:\ x^{2}+y^{2}\leq A^{2}\},

and, for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the functions

(10.95)

\displaystyle h_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y):=\max\{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}},1\},\qquad g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y):=h_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)^{-\alpha}.

We have the trivial bounds
A^{-\alpha}\leq g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)\leq 1
for all
(x,y)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
. Moreover, for
p=\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}y)\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
,

(10.96)

\displaystyle V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)=\kappa^{-\alpha/2}g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y).

Starting from
u_{\omega}
defined in (
10.5
), we introduce the rescaled two-step process

(10.97)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,y):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\left((f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}y)))_{x},\,(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}y)))_{y}\right),

which can be rewritten as

(10.98)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,y)=\kappa^{-1/2}\bigl((\phi_{2}^{\kappa})_{x},(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})_{y}\bigr),

where
\phi_{2}^{\kappa}
is the solution at time
2
of (
10.6
) with initial datum
\phi_{0}^{\kappa}=\Psi_{\mathsf{z}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}y)
.
From (
10.96
) we immediately have the following equivalence result for
(x,y)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
.

Lemma 10.17
.

Let
A>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.99)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
with
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
;

(2)

there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.100)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,y))]\leq\gamma g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
(x,y)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
.

The equations for the rescaled process
\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{t}=(\widetilde{x}^{\kappa}_{t},\widetilde{y}^{\kappa}_{t})
are obtained from (
10.6
) and read, for
t\in[0,1)
and
t\in[1,2)
respectively,

(10.101)

\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{x}_{t}^{\kappa}&=\left(-\omega_{1}\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}-2\kappa\widetilde{x}_{t}^{\kappa}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{1}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{B}}_{t},\\
d\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}&=\left(\omega_{1}\widetilde{x}_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}-2\kappa\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{2}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{B}}_{t},\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{x}_{t}^{\kappa}&=\left(\omega_{2}\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}z_{t}^{\kappa}-2\kappa\widetilde{x}_{t}^{\kappa}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{1}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t},\\
d\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}&=-2\kappa\widetilde{y}_{t}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}e_{2}\cdot\Pi_{\phi_{t}^{\kappa}}\mathrm{d}\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{t}={\mathbf{B}}_{t}-{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
for
t\in[1,2)
and
\Pi_{\phi^{\kappa}_{t}}
is as in (
10.32
). On the other hand, the limiting rescaled process
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}=(\widetilde{x}_{t},\widetilde{y}_{t})
satisfies, again for
t\in[0,1)
and
t\in[1,2)
respectively

(10.102)

\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{x}_{t}&=-\omega_{1}\widetilde{y}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{1},\\
d\widetilde{y}_{t}&=\omega_{1}\widetilde{x}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{2},\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} d\widetilde{x}_{t}&=\omega_{2}\widetilde{y}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{B}_{t}^{1},\\
d\widetilde{y}_{t}&=\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{B}_{t}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

As in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
section, we next compare the exact rescaled process
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}:=\widetilde{\phi}^{\kappa}_{2}
with a limiting rescaled process
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}:=\widetilde{\phi}_{2}
.

Lemma 10.18
.

Let
A,N>1
. There exists a constant
C_{A,N}>0
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
and all
(x,y)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
there holds

(10.103)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,y)-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y)|\bigr]\leq C_{A,N}\kappa^{1/4}

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

The proof is identical in structure to that of Lemma
10.5
. For the rescaled trajectory
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}
define the stopping time

(10.104)

\displaystyle\tau_{\kappa}:=\inf\{t\in[0,2]:|\widetilde{\phi}_{t}^{\kappa}|\geq\kappa^{-1/4}\}\wedge 2.

On
[0,\tau_{\kappa}]
, the spherical trajectory remains in the north chart and
z_{t}^{\kappa}\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Consequently the exact rescaled coefficients are
O(\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}})
-perturbations of the limiting coefficients in (
10.101
), uniformly in
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
. A standard Burkholder–Davis–Gundy argument and Grönwall’s inequality then yields the analogue of (
10.40
).
Combining this with the same maximal moment bound used in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
case and the estimate
{\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{\kappa}<2)\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}
, one obtains the claim exactly as in the proof of Lemma
10.5
.
∎

Proposition 10.19
.

Let
\alpha,\gamma\in(0,1)
and
A,N>1
. Then there exists
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\alpha,\gamma,A,N)>0
such that

(10.105)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,y))-g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))|\bigr]\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)

for all
(x,y)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Proposition
10.6
, since
g_{\mathsf{z}}
is globally Lipschitz on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)\geq A^{-\alpha}
on
K_{A}^{\mathsf{z}}
.
∎

Moving on to determine a drift for the limiting process, we record that the explicit solution of the first limiting map can be written for all
t\in[0,1]
as

(10.106)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{x}_{t},\widetilde{y}_{t})=R_{\omega_{1}}(x,y)+G^{(1)},\qquad R_{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&-\sin\theta\\
\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix},

and
G^{(1)}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,2I_{2})
is independent of
\omega_{1}
. For the second map,

(10.107)

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{2},

(10.108)

\displaystyle\widetilde{x}_{2}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{x}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{1}+\omega_{2}\left(\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s\right).

We will use this to mirror the same annular/inner-core decomposition as in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
section.

10.2.2.
The annular core
K_{A_{1}}^{\mathsf{z}}\setminus K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{z}}

We begin with the large-rescaled region.

Lemma 10.20
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exists
C_{\alpha}>0
such that for all
h>0
, all
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|c|\leq h/2
, all
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and all
N\geq 1
,

(10.109)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega}\left[\min\{1,(N|h\sin(\omega+\theta)+c|)^{-\alpha}\}\right]\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(Nh)^{-\alpha}\},

where
\omega\sim\mathrm{Unif}([-N,N])
.

Proof.

First, the constant upper bound is immediate for any
C_{\alpha}>1
. Setting

(10.110)

I:=\frac{1}{2N}\int_{-N}^{N}\min\{1,(N|h\sin(\omega+\theta)+c|)^{-\alpha}\}\,{\rm d}\omega,

we immediately have from
\min\{1,x^{-\alpha}\}\leq x^{-\alpha}
that

(10.111)

I\leq N^{-\alpha}h^{-\alpha}\frac{1}{2N}\int_{-N}^{N}\bigl|\sin(\omega+\theta)+a\bigr|^{-\alpha}\,{\rm d}\omega,\qquad a:=\frac{c}{h}.

Using that the density of
(\omega+\theta)\!\mod 2\pi
is uniformly bounded in
N
and
\theta
, the integral further reduces to

(10.112)

I\leq C_{0}N^{-\alpha}h^{-\alpha}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\sin(u)+a|^{-\alpha}\,{\rm d}u.

By assumptions,
|a|\leq\frac{1}{2}
implies that the integral can be bounded uniformly in
a
.
Indeed for each such
a
, the function
u\mapsto\sin u+a
has exactly two zeros
u_{1}(a),u_{2}(a)
in
[0,2\pi)
, and these are uniformly non-degenerate since

(10.113)

|\cos u_{j}(a)|=\sqrt{1-a^{2}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\qquad j=1,2.

Hence, fixing
\delta:=\sqrt{3}/4
, the mean value theorem gives

(10.114)

|\sin u+a|\geq\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4}\,|u-u_{j}(a)|

whenever
|u-u_{j}(a)|\leq\delta
, so the contribution of each
\delta
-neighbourhood is bounded by a constant depending only on
\alpha
, since
\alpha<1
.
On the complement of these two neighbourhoods, the continuous function
(a,u)\mapsto|\sin u+a|
is strictly positive on a compact set, hence bounded below by some
m>0
uniformly in
|a|\leq 1/2
. Therefore the remaining contribution is bounded by
2\pi m^{-\alpha}
. This proves

(10.115)

\sup_{|a|\leq 1/2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\sin u+a|^{-\alpha}\,{\rm d}u<\infty,

and concludes the proof.
∎

We now determine the threshold
A_{0}>1
such that
g_{\mathsf{z}}
satisfies a local Lyapunov drift condition for the limiting rescaled dynamics in the annular region
(K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{z}})^{c}
.

Proposition 10.21
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
A_{0}=A_{0}(\alpha)>1
and
N_{\infty}=N_{\infty}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
N\geq N_{\infty}
and every
(x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with
h_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)\geq A_{0}
, there holds

(10.116)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))]\leq\frac{1}{2}\,g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y).

Proof.

Fix
(x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and let
h_{0}:=h_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)\geq 1
. Since
h_{0}\geq A_{0}>1
, we may write
(x,y)=h_{0}(\cos\phi,\sin\phi)
for some
\phi\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\widetilde{x}_{2}=\Theta_{2}+\omega_{2}\Theta_{1}
after setting

(10.117)

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}:=\widetilde{y}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{B}_{s}^{2}\,{\rm d}s,\qquad\Theta_{2}:=\widetilde{x}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\,\overline{B}_{1}^{1}.

Since
g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{x}_{2},\widetilde{y}_{2})\leq\min\{1,|\widetilde{x}_{2}|^{-\alpha}\}
, the same one-dimensional averaging argument used in the proof of Lemma
10.7
implies the existence of
C_{1,\alpha}>0
such that

(10.118)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{2}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))]\leq C_{1,\alpha}\,\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\},

thus,

(10.119)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))]\leq C_{1,\alpha}\,{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\bigr].

Next, using the representation of
\widetilde{y}_{1}
, we may write

(10.120)

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}=h_{0}\sin(\omega_{1}+\phi)+G,

where
G
is a centred Gaussian random variable, independent of
\omega_{1}
, with variance independent of
h_{0}
.
On
F:=\{|G|\leq h_{0}/2\}
, Lemma
10.20
yields

(10.121)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},{\mathbf{B}}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Theta_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\,\big|\,F\Bigr]\leq C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}.

On the other hand, since
G
is Gaussian, there exist universal constants
c,C>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})\leq Ce^{-ch_{0}^{2}}
. In particular, we may choose
A_{0}>1
large enough so that
C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})\leq\frac{1}{4}h_{0}^{-\alpha}
for all
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
. Summing up, for
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
,

(10.122)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}+C_{1,\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(F^{c})

(10.123)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}+\frac{1}{4}h_{0}^{-\alpha}.

Choosing
N_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}
so large that
C_{1,\alpha}C_{2,\alpha}N^{-\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for all
N\geq N_{\infty}
, the proposition is proved.
∎

10.2.3.
The inner core
K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{z}}

Once
A_{0}
is determined by Proposition
10.21
above, we
now turn to studying the dynamics in the core
K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{z}}
.

Proposition 10.22
.

Let
A_{0}>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. There exists
N_{0}=N_{0}(A_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.124)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[g_{\mathsf{z}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,y))]\leq\gamma g_{\mathsf{z}}(x,y)

for all
N\geq N_{0}
and all
(x,y)\in K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{z}}
.

The proof is analogous to that of Proposition
10.11
, since the last motion of
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
is shearing.
In view of Propositions
10.19
,
10.21
,
10.22
, and Lemma
10.17
, the following result is immediate.

Corollary 10.23
.

Let
A_{1}>A_{0}
. Then there exist
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.125)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
satisfying
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\leq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

10.2.4.
Inviscid-dominated regime

We consider here the region where the inviscid dynamics dominate the stochastic processes by assuming that
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\geq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
, for some
A_{1}\geq A_{0}
to be determined.
We show that a version of Lemma
5.8
holds for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
.

Remark 10.24
.

The invariance of
r_{\mathsf{z}}
via the first rotation simplifies the
F_{\mathsf{z}}
analysis relative to the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
in a fundamental way.
Near
F_{\mathsf{x}}
, the first deterministic step
f_{\omega_{1}}^{3}
rotates the
(x,y)
-plane and thereby mixes the coordinates of
r_{\mathsf{x}}=\max\{|y|,|z|\}
, so the good event must control both
|\phi_{1}^{\kappa}-\phi_{1}|
and
|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|
.
Near
F_{\mathsf{z}}
, the first step leaves
r_{\mathsf{z}}
invariant deterministically for every realisation of
\omega_{1}
.
As a result, the three-case decomposition can be defined entirely in terms of the deterministic midpoint
\phi_{1}
(for which
r_{\mathsf{z}}(\phi_{1})=r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)
exactly), and the only perturbation that must be controlled explicitly is the endpoint deviation
|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|
.
The good event therefore reduces to a single condition.

Proposition 10.25
.

Fix
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
. Then there exist
N>1
,
A>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\kappa_{0}>0
, and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.126)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\right]\leq\gamma\,V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{z}}(s_{0})
with
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\geq A\sqrt{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Fix a small
\eta>0
(to be determined) and define the good set

(10.127)

G:=\{|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|\leq\eta r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\}.

On the good event
G
we follow the splitting presented in Lemma
5.8
, recalling that

(10.128)

E_{1}=\{N^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{1}|\leq y_{1}\},\quad E_{2}=\left\{\frac{|x_{1}|}{2N}\leq y_{1}\leq\frac{|x_{1}|}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\},\quad E_{3}=\left\{0<y_{1}<\frac{|x_{1}|}{2N}\right\},

and further refining
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
into

(10.129)

E_{1,1}=\left\{|\omega_{2}|>2\frac{M+1}{1-\delta_{0}}N^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\quad E_{1,2}=E_{1,1}^{c},\quad E_{2,1}=\left\{|x_{2}|\leq\frac{y_{1}}{2}\right\},\quad E_{2,2}=E_{2,1}^{c},

where
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
and
M>0
will be determined later. Let
r_{2}=r_{\mathsf{z}}(p_{2})
and
r_{1}=r_{\mathsf{z}}(p_{1})
, with
p_{2}=\phi_{2}
and
p_{1}=\phi_{1}
. We recall that

–

On
E_{1,1}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{1+M^{2}N}{1+N}}r_{1}\geq\frac{M}{2}r_{1}
for
N\geq 1
.

–

On
E_{1,2}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r_{1}}{\sqrt{1+N}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1,2})=2\frac{M+1}{1-\delta_{0}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

–

On
E_{2}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r_{1}}{\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{2})\leq C_{2}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for some universal
C_{2}>0
.

–

On
E_{3}
there holds
r_{2}\geq\frac{r_{1}}{4}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{3})\leq C_{3}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for some universal
C_{3}>0
.

and we set
E:=E_{1,1}
and
E^{c}=E_{1,2}\cup E_{2}\cup E_{3}
. Since
|r_{\mathsf{z}}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})-r_{\mathsf{z}}(\phi_{2})|\leq 4|\phi_{2}^{\kappa}-\phi_{2}|\leq 4\eta r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)
we now have

(10.130)

\displaystyle r_{2}^{\kappa}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{M}{2}-4\eta\right)r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\mathbf{1}_{E}+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}-4\eta\right)r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}

(10.131)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{M}{4}r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\mathbf{1}_{E}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}

for
4\eta=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}
and
M>4
. For
M=8
we have
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and

(10.132)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{B}},\omega}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\mathbf{1}_{G}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-\alpha}V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)+(2\sqrt{1+4N^{2}})^{\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

(10.133)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

choosing
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
and
N>1
large enough, for some
\gamma\in(2^{-\alpha},1)
. For
G^{c}
, we argue as in Proposition
10.13
to conclude that

(10.134)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\!\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})\mathbf{1}_{G^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{N}}{\eta^{2}A^{2-\alpha}}V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

for
A
large enough. With this, the proof is completed.
∎

We can now conclude the local and global Lyapunov estimates, as done for the fixed point
F_{\mathsf{x}}
in Section
10.1
.

Proposition 10.26
.

There exist
N>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.135)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof of Proposition
10.15
.

Combining Proposition
10.26
and Lemma
10.16
, according to whether
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})
or not, yields

(10.136)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)+C\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{{\mathsf{z}}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{X}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.
∎

10.3.
Stochastic drift function for
F_{\mathsf{y}}
fixed points

We now turn to the fixed points
F_{\mathsf{y}}=\{(0,\pm 1,0)\}
and throughout this subsection we work near
(0,1,0)
, the point
(0,-1,0)
being treated identically by symmetry.
As in the deterministic construction of Lemma
5.9
, the natural radial quantity is not measured directly at
p
, but after the fixed post-rotation
f_{N}^{3}
.
We keep the notation
f_{\omega}^{\kappa},f_{\omega},\phi_{1}^{\kappa},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}^{\kappa},\phi_{2}
introduced in Section
10.1
and we further define
r_{\mathsf{y}}(p):=\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}}
for
p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
and the functions

(10.137)

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p):=\max\{r_{\mathsf{y}}(p),\sqrt{\kappa}\}^{-\alpha},\qquad V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p):=\widetilde{V}_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{N}^{3}(p)),

for some
N>1
and some
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
to be determined later. The local chart around
F_{\mathsf{y}}
reads

(10.138)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z):=(x,\sqrt{1-x^{2}-z^{2}},z),\qquad\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0}):=\Psi_{\mathsf{y}}(\{(x,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\;:\;\ 0<x^{2}+z^{2}<s_{0}^{2}\}).

The goal of this subsection is the following Lyapunov–Foster drift estimate.

Proposition 10.27
.

There exist
N>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta_{\mathsf{y}}\geq 1
, and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.139)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)+\beta_{\mathsf{y}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{X}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

As in Section
10.1
, the proof is divided according to the size of
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p))
relative to the stochastic scale
\sqrt{\kappa}
.
We first record the stability in expectation of the noisy two-step map after the fixed post-rotation.

Lemma 10.28
.

There exists
C_{N}=C(N)>0
such that

(10.140)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\bigl|f_{N}^{3}(\phi_{1}^{\kappa})-f_{N}^{3}(\phi_{1})\bigr|^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\bigl|f_{N}^{3}(\phi_{2}^{\kappa})-f_{N}^{3}(\phi_{2})\bigr|^{2}\leq C_{N}\kappa

for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
, all
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, and all
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in[-N,N]^{2}
.

Proof.

Since
f_{N}^{3}:{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\to{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
is a smooth diffeomorphism, its derivative is uniformly bounded for fixed
N
.
Hence, there exists
C_{N}>0
such that

(10.141)

\displaystyle\bigl|f_{N}^{3}(a)-f_{N}^{3}(b)\bigr|\leq C_{N}|a-b|,

for all
a,b\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
.
Applying this with
a=\phi_{1}^{\kappa}
,
b=\phi_{1}
, and then with
a=\phi_{2}^{\kappa}
,
b=\phi_{2}
, and using Lemma
10.1
, proves the claim.
∎

We next obtain a uniform bound away from the
F_{\mathsf{y}}
caps.

Lemma 10.29
.

There exists
C>0
such that

(10.142)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq C

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})^{c}
and all
\kappa\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

The proof follows as in Lemma
10.3
by considering the map
(p,\omega)\longmapsto r_{\mathsf{y}}\bigl(f_{N}^{3}(f_{\omega}(p))\bigr)
.
∎

10.3.1.
Stochastic regime

We now treat the regime in which
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p))\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}
.
As in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
and
F_{\mathsf{z}}
analysis, we pass to rescaled variables, but here the rescaling is performed after conjugation by
f_{N}^{3}
.

For
A>1
, define

(10.143)

\displaystyle K_{A}^{\mathsf{y}}:=\{(x,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:\ \sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}}\leq A\},

and, for some
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,

(10.144)

\displaystyle g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z):=h_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)^{-\alpha},\qquad h_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z):=\max\{\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}},1\},

hence
A^{-\alpha}\leq g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)\leq 1
for all
(x,z)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{y}}
. If
\overline{p}=f_{N}^{3}(p)=\Psi_{\mathsf{y}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}z)
, we have

(10.145)

\displaystyle V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)=\kappa^{-\alpha/2}g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z).

We introduce the corresponding rescaled two-step process

(10.146)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,z):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\left(\bigl(f_{N}^{3}\circ f_{\omega}^{\kappa}\circ f_{-N}^{3}(\Psi_{\mathsf{y}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}z))\bigr)_{x},\,\bigl(f_{N}^{3}\circ f_{\omega}^{\kappa}\circ f_{-N}^{3}(\Psi_{\mathsf{y}}(\sqrt{\kappa}x,\sqrt{\kappa}z))\bigr)_{z}\right).

For this process, the following equivalence holds, whose proof is identical to the one of Lemma
10.4
.

Lemma 10.30
.

Let
A>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.147)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})
such that
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p))\leq A\sqrt{\kappa}
;

(2)

there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.148)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,z))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)

for all sufficiently small
\kappa
and all
(x,z)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{y}}
.

We next identify the limiting rescaled process, in the spirit of Sections
10.1
and
10.2
. For convenience we write

(10.149)

\displaystyle G_{1}:=\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{1},\qquad G_{2}:=\sqrt{2}\,B_{1}^{3},\qquad G_{3}:=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{1}B_{s}^{3}\,{\rm d}s,

where
B^{1},B^{3}
are independent Brownian motions, so that
G_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2)
,
G_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2)
, and
G_{3}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2/3)
.

Lemma 10.31
.

Let
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\sim\mathrm{Unif}([-N,N])
be independent. Let
(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})
be as above and let
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2I_{2})
, independent of
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})
. Then, for every
(x,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, the limiting rescaled map
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z)=(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{z})
is given by

(10.150)

\displaystyle\widetilde{x}_{1}

\displaystyle:=x+(N-\omega_{1})z+G_{1}-\omega_{1}G_{3},

(10.151)

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}_{1}

\displaystyle:=z+G_{2},

and

(10.152)

\displaystyle\widetilde{x}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{x}_{1}-N\widetilde{z}_{1})\cos(\omega_{2})+(N\widetilde{x}_{1}+\widetilde{z}_{1})\sin(\omega_{2})+(\xi_{1}-N\xi_{2}),

(10.153)

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{x}_{1}\sin(\omega_{2})+\widetilde{z}_{1}\cos(\omega_{2})+\xi_{2}.

In particular, there exist random variables
\rho_{1}\geq 0
,
\theta_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\zeta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2+2N^{2})
, with
\zeta
independent of
(\omega_{2},\rho_{1},\theta_{1})
, such that

(10.154)

\displaystyle\widetilde{x}=\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\,\rho_{1}\sin(\omega_{2}+\theta_{1})+\zeta,\qquad\rho_{1}:=\sqrt{\widetilde{x}_{1}^{2}+\widetilde{z}_{1}^{2}}.

Proof.

We argue analogously as in the previous section, using the explicit form of
u_{\omega}
together with the conjugation
f_{-N}^{3}
, the exact rescaling and the resulting limit obtained for
\kappa\to 0
.
The representation (
10.154
) follows from the identity

(10.155)

\displaystyle\bigl(\widetilde{x}_{1}-N\widetilde{z}_{1}\bigr)^{2}+\bigl(N\widetilde{x}_{1}+\widetilde{z}_{1}\bigr)^{2}=(1+N^{2})\bigl(\widetilde{x}_{1}^{2}+\widetilde{z}_{1}^{2}\bigr),

together with the independence of
\xi
.
∎

We now split the stochastic core into annular and inner zones.

10.3.2.
The annular core
K_{A_{1}}^{\mathsf{y}}\setminus K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{y}}

Unlike the corresponding annular-core analysis in the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
and
F_{\mathsf{z}}
regimes, after conjugation by
f_{N}^{3}
the quantity
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(\cdot))
is no longer adapted to a single coordinate of the rescaled limiting dynamics.
In particular, the deterministic component of the map does not reduce to the same simple shear/rotation structure used previously, and so the proof cannot be obtained by a direct repetition of the arguments from the other sections.
To recover a quantitative drift estimate, we instead exploit more explicitly the Gaussian structure of the limiting rescaled process: the relevant radial variable can be written in terms of linear combinations of Gaussian random variables, and this allows us to control both the averaged rotation effect and the Gaussian tails that appear in the annular regime.
The following two preliminary lemmas isolate precisely these probabilistic features and will be the key ingredients in the proof of Proposition
10.33
.

Lemma 10.32

(Gaussian smoothing estimates)
.

Fix
0<\sigma_{-}\leq\sigma_{+}<\infty
, and let
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
with

\sigma\in[\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}]
. Then,

(i)

For every
\alpha\in(0,1)
, there exists
C_{\alpha,\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}}>0
, depending only on
\alpha,\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}
, such that for all
\lambda\geq 1
and all
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(10.156)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\min\{1,(\lambda|a+G|)^{-\alpha}\}\bigr]\leq C_{\alpha,\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}}\min\{1,(\lambda|a|)^{-\alpha}\}.

(ii)

For every
c_{0}>0
, there exists
c_{1}=c_{1}(c_{0},\sigma_{+})>0
such that for all
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(10.157)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[e^{-c_{0}(a+G)^{2}}\bigr]\leq e^{-c_{1}a^{2}}.

Proof.

We start by proving (i). Since
\min\{1,(\lambda|x|)^{-\alpha}\}\leq\lambda^{-\alpha}|x|^{-\alpha}
, it is enough to obtain

(10.158)

{\mathbb{E}}[|a+G|^{-\alpha}]\leq C_{\alpha,\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}}\min\{1,|a|^{-\alpha}\},

uniformly for
\sigma\in[\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}]
. Indeed, for
\lambda\geq 1
, it holds

(10.159)

{\mathbb{E}}[\min\{1,(\lambda|a+G|)^{-\alpha}\}]\leq\lambda^{-\alpha}{\mathbb{E}}[|a+G|^{-\alpha}]\leq C_{\alpha}\lambda^{-\alpha}\min\{1,|a|^{-\alpha}\}\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(\lambda|a|)^{-\alpha}\}.

To prove (
10.158
) set
t:=\frac{a}{\sigma}
and write

(10.160)

{\mathbb{E}}[|a+G|^{-\alpha}]=\sigma^{-\alpha}F_{\alpha}(t),\qquad F_{\alpha}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u|^{-\alpha}\varphi_{1}(u-t)\,{\rm d}u,

where
\varphi_{1}(v):=(2\pi)^{-1/2}e^{-v^{2}/2}
denotes the density of
\sigma^{-1}G\sim N(0,1)
.
It is easy to check that for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(10.161)

F_{\alpha}(t)\leq C_{\alpha}(1+|t|)^{-\alpha}.

Indeed, when
|t|\leq 2
, splitting the integration whether
|u|\leq 4
or not and using integrability of
|u|^{-\alpha}
near zero and
\varphi_{1}
near
\infty
respectively, shows that
F_{\alpha}(t)\leq C_{\alpha}
uniformly. On the other hand, when
|t|>2
, integrating where
|u|>|t|/2
or not and again using the monotonicity and integrability properties of
|u|^{-\alpha}
and
\varphi_{1}(t)
respectively proves that
F_{\alpha}(t)\leq C_{\alpha}|t|^{-\alpha}
.

Consequently,

(10.162)

{\mathbb{E}}[|a+G|^{-\alpha}]\leq C_{\alpha}\sigma^{-\alpha}(1+|a|/\sigma)^{-\alpha}=C_{\alpha}(\sigma+|a|)^{-\alpha}\leq C_{\alpha,\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}}\min\{1,|a|^{-\alpha}\}

and using
\sigma\in[\sigma_{-},\sigma_{+}]
, we obtain (
10.158
).

For point (ii), a direct Gaussian computation yields

(10.163)

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[e^{-c_{0}(a+G)^{2}}\bigr]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+2c_{0}\sigma^{2}}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{c_{0}}{1+2c_{0}\sigma^{2}}a^{2}\right)\leq e^{-c_{1}a^{2}},

where we used that
\sigma\leq\sigma_{+}
.
∎

We are now in position to prove a drift estimate for the rescaled process.

Proposition 10.33
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
A_{0}=A_{0}(\alpha)>1
and
N_{\infty}=N_{\infty}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for every
N\geq N_{\infty}
and every
(x,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with
h_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)\geq A_{0}
, there holds

(10.164)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr]\leq\frac{1}{2}\,g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z).

Proof.

Fix
(x,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, and set
h_{0}:=h_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)=\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}}
.
Since
h_{0}\geq A_{0}>1
, we have
g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)=h_{0}^{-\alpha}
and
g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{z})\leq\min\{1,|\widetilde{x}|^{-\alpha}\}
, so it suffices to estimate
\widetilde{x}
.
From Lemma
10.31
recall that

(10.165)

\widetilde{x}=\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\,\rho_{1}\sin(\omega_{2}+\theta_{1})+\zeta,\qquad\rho_{1}:=\sqrt{\widetilde{x}_{1}^{2}+\widetilde{z}_{1}^{2}},

where

(10.166)

\widetilde{x}_{1}=x+(N-\omega_{1})z+G_{1}-\omega_{1}G_{3},\qquad\widetilde{z}_{1}=z+G_{2},

and
\zeta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2+2N^{2})
is independent of
(\omega_{2},\rho_{1},\theta_{1})
, and define the good event

(10.167)

F_{1}:=\Bigl\{|\zeta|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\,\rho_{1}\Bigr\}.

Conditioning on
\mathcal{H}_{5}:=\sigma(\omega_{1},G_{1},G_{2},G_{3},\zeta)
, on
F_{1}
we write

(10.168)

\widetilde{x}=N\Bigl(h\sin(\omega_{2}+\theta_{1})+c\Bigr),\qquad h:=\frac{\sqrt{1+N^{2}}}{N}\rho_{1},\qquad c:=\frac{\zeta}{N},

and we have
|c|\leq h/2
. Hence, by Lemma
10.20
,
\rho_{1}\geq|\widetilde{x}_{1}|
and
\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\geq N
,

(10.169)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{2}}\Bigl[\min\{1,|\widetilde{x}|^{-\alpha}\}\,\Big|\,\mathcal{H}_{5}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,N^{-\alpha}h^{-\alpha}\}

(10.170)

\displaystyle=C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\,\rho_{1})^{-\alpha}\}

(10.171)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}.

On the complement
F_{1}^{c}
, for
\mathcal{H}_{4}=\sigma(\omega_{1},G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})
the Gaussian tail of
\zeta/\sqrt{1+N^{2}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,2)
leads to

(10.172)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(F_{1}^{c}\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}_{4}\bigr)\leq Ce^{-c\rho_{1}^{2}},

so that, using
g_{\mathsf{y}}\leq 1
on
F_{1}^{c}
,

(10.173)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{2},\zeta}\Bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\,\Big|\,\mathcal{H}_{4}\Bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}+Ce^{-c\rho_{1}^{2}}.

We now treat separately the two terms appearing in (
10.173
) starting with the first one. Write
\mathcal{H}_{2}=\sigma(G_{1},G_{3})
and

(10.174)

\widetilde{x}_{1}=\Lambda_{1}+\omega_{1}\Lambda_{2},\qquad\Lambda_{1}:=x+Nz+G_{1},\qquad\Lambda_{2}:=-(z+G_{3}),

so that an application of Lemma
10.7
gives

(10.175)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1}}\Bigl[\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\,\Big|\,\mathcal{H}_{2}\Bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}\min\bigl\{1,(N|\Lambda_{1}|)^{-\alpha},(N^{2}|\Lambda_{2}|)^{-\alpha}\bigr\}.

Taking expectation in
G_{1},G_{3}
and using

(10.176)

\min\{1,(N|\Lambda_{1}|)^{-\alpha},(N^{2}|\Lambda_{2}|)^{-\alpha}\}\leq\min\{1,(N|\Lambda_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\},

as well as the analogous bound with
\Lambda_{2}
, we obtain

(10.177)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr]\Bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}\min\Bigl\{1,\,{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\Lambda_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\bigr],\,{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N^{2}|\Lambda_{2}|)^{-\alpha}\}\bigr]\Bigr\}.

Since
\Lambda_{1}=x+Nz+G_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}=-(z+G_{3})
, point (i) from Lemma
10.32
with exponent
\alpha
yields

(10.178)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr]\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\min\bigl\{1,(N|x+Nz|)^{-\alpha},(N^{2}|z|)^{-\alpha}\bigr\}

(10.179)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\min\bigl\{(N|x+Nz|)^{-\alpha},(N^{2}|z|)^{-\alpha}\bigr\}

(10.180)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}2^{\alpha}N^{-\alpha}\Bigl(|x+Nz|+N|z|\Bigr)^{-\alpha}

as both
G_{1}
and
G_{3}
have uniformly bounded variance. Since
N\geq 1
,

(10.181)

h_{0}=\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}}\leq|x|+|z|\leq|x+Nz|+(N+1)|z|\leq 2(|x+Nz|+N|z|),

therefore,

(10.182)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1}}\bigl[\min\{1,(N|\widetilde{x}_{1}|)^{-\alpha}\}\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr]\Bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}.

We move to the second term in (
10.173
), and we use the trivial bound

(10.183)

e^{-c\rho_{1}^{2}}\leq e^{-c(\Lambda_{1}+\omega_{1}\Lambda_{2})^{2}}\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}\min\{1,|\Lambda_{1}+\omega_{1}\Lambda_{2}|^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}}\},\qquad{\alpha^{\prime}}=\frac{1+\alpha}{2}<1

to deduce from Lemma
10.7

(10.184)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},G_{2}}\bigl[e^{-c\rho_{1}^{2}}\,\big|\,\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr]\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}\min\{1,|\Lambda_{1}|^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}},(N|\Lambda_{2}|)^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}}\},

and, mimicking the argument above, we infer

(10.185)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega_{1},G_{1},G_{2},G_{3}}\bigl[e^{-c\rho_{1}^{2}}\bigr]\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}h_{0}^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}}.

Finally, combining (
10.173
), (
10.182
), and (
10.185
), we conclude that

(10.186)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr]\leq C_{\alpha}N^{-\alpha}h_{0}^{-\alpha}+C_{{\alpha^{\prime}}}h_{0}^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}}.

Since
{\alpha^{\prime}}>\alpha
, there exists
A_{0}=A_{0}(\alpha)>1
such that, for all
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
,

(10.187)

C_{{\alpha^{\prime}}}h_{0}^{-{\alpha^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{1}{4}h_{0}^{-\alpha},

and, once
A_{0}
is fixed, choose
N_{\infty}=N_{\infty}(\alpha)
so large that, for all
N\geq N_{\infty}
,

(10.188)

C_{\alpha}N^{-\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Then, for every
N\geq N_{\infty}
and every
h_{0}\geq A_{0}
,

(10.189)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr]\leq\frac{1}{2}h_{0}^{-\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z).

This proves the proposition.
∎

10.3.3.
The inner core
K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{y}}

Once
A_{0}
is fixed by Proposition
10.33
, the dynamics inside
K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{y}}
are handled by the direct Gaussian spreading mechanism.

Proposition 10.34
.

Let
A_{0}>1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then there exist
N_{0}=N_{0}(A_{0},\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.190)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)

for all
N\geq N_{0}
and all
(x,z)\in K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{y}}
.

Proof.

Fix
(x,z)\in K_{A_{0}}^{\mathsf{y}}
and set
h_{0}:=h_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)\in[1,A_{0}]
so that
g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)=h_{0}^{-\alpha}
and
A_{0}^{-\alpha}\leq g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)\leq 1
. For
(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{z})=\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z)
define

(10.191)

\displaystyle E:=\{|\widetilde{x}|>2h_{0}\},\qquad E^{c}=\{|\widetilde{x}|\leq 2h_{0}\}.

On
E
, we have
g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{z})\leq 2^{-\alpha}g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)
, while conditionally on

(10.192)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}:=\sigma\bigl(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},G_{1},G_{2},G_{3}\bigr),

Lemma
10.31
implies that
\widetilde{x}\,|\,\mathcal{H}\sim\mathcal{N}(a,2+2N^{2})
for some
\mathcal{H}
-measurable
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Therefore

(10.193)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{H})\leq\frac{4h_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi(2+2N^{2})}}\leq\frac{4h_{0}}{N},

and we note that taking expectations removes the conditioning on
\mathcal{H}
.
Using the trivial bound
g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{z})\leq 1\leq A_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)
, we conclude

(10.194)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))|E\bigr]{\mathbb{P}}(E)+{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))|E^{c}\bigr]{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})

(10.195)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2^{-\alpha}+4\frac{A_{0}^{1+\alpha}}{N}\right)g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z).

Choosing
