N_{0}=N_{0}(A_{0},\alpha)
so large that the coefficient is strictly smaller than
1
proves the result.
∎

We next compare the exact rescaled process
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}
with the limiting
process
\widetilde{f}_{\omega}
, obtaining the same result as Lemma
10.5
. We omit the proof.

Lemma 10.35
.

Let
A,N>1
. There exists
C_{A,N}>0
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.196)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\bigl|\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,z)-\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z)\bigr|\Bigr]\leq C_{A,N}\kappa^{1/4}

for all
(x,z)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{y}}
, all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
, and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

The following proposition is the
F_{\mathsf{y}}
version of Proposition
10.6
.

Proposition 10.36
.

Let
\alpha,\gamma\in(0,1)
,
A>1
, and
N>1
. Then there exists
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\alpha,\gamma,A,N)>0
such that

(10.197)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\bigl|g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,z))-g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}(x,z))\bigr|\Bigr]\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\,g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)

for all
(x,z)\in K_{A}^{\mathsf{y}}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

In view of Propositions
10.33
,
10.34
, and
10.36
, the following result is immediate.

Corollary 10.37
.

Let
A_{1}>A_{0}
. Then there exist
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.198)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[g_{\mathsf{y}}(\widetilde{f}_{\omega}^{\kappa}(x,z))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,g_{\mathsf{y}}(x,z)

for all
(x,z)\in K_{A_{1}}^{\mathsf{y}}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Combining Corollary
10.37
with Lemma
10.30
yields the drift in the physical stochastic core.

Proposition 10.38
.

Let
A_{1}>A_{0}
. Then there exist
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.199)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})
satisfying
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p))\leq A_{1}\sqrt{\kappa}
and for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

10.3.4.
Inviscid-dominated regime

We first treat the regime in which the stochastic dynamics
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
can be seen as a small perturbation of the inviscid dynamics
f_{\omega}
.

Proposition 10.39
.

Fix
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
. Then there exist
N>1
,
A>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\kappa_{0}>0
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(10.200)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
and all
p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
such that
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})
and
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p))\geq A\sqrt{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Let
p_{0}\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
,
\overline{p}=f_{N}^{3}(p_{0})
and
q_{\omega_{1}}=f_{\omega_{1}}^{3}(p_{0})
. Hence,
p_{0}=f_{-N}^{3}(\overline{p})
,
q_{\omega_{1}}=f_{\omega_{1}-N}^{3}(\overline{p})
,
{V}_{\mathsf{y}}(p_{0})=\widetilde{V}_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})
, and
{V}_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{\omega}(p_{0}))=\widetilde{V}_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}\circ f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}(q_{\omega_{1}}))
. Let
p_{2}^{\kappa}=f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p_{0})
and
p_{2}=f_{\omega}(p_{0})
and define the set

(10.201)

\displaystyle G:=\{|r_{{\mathsf{y}}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}^{\kappa}))-r_{{\mathsf{y}}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))|\leq\eta r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})\},

for some
\eta>0
to be chosen later on. Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.8
,
f_{N}^{3}(p_{2})=f_{N}^{3}\circ f_{\omega_{2}}^{2}(q_{\omega_{1}})
, and we see that

–

On
E_{1,1}^{\omega_{2}}
we have
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))\geq\sqrt{\frac{1+M^{2}N}{1+N}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})
. Note that
E_{1,2}
is empty since we are choosing
\omega_{3}=N
.

–

On
E_{2}^{\omega_{2}}
we have
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})}{\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{2}}(E_{2}^{\omega_{2}})\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{N}}
.

–

On
E_{3}^{\omega_{2}}
we have
r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})}{4}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{2}}(E_{3}^{\omega_{2}})\leq\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{N}}
.

On the other hand,
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})=r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{\widetilde{\omega}_{1}}^{3}\circ\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))
for
\widetilde{\omega}_{1}=N-\omega_{1}\in[0,2N]
and thus, again as in Lemma
5.8
, we have

–

On
E_{1,1}^{\omega_{1}}
there holds
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})\geq\sqrt{\frac{1+M^{2}N}{1+N}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))
.

–

On
E_{1,2}^{\omega_{1}}
there holds
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))}{\sqrt{1+N}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{1}}(E_{1,2}^{\omega_{1}})=2\frac{M+1}{1-\delta_{0}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

–

On
E_{2,1}^{\omega_{1}}
there holds
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))}{\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{1}}(E_{2,1}^{\omega_{1}})\leq N^{-1}
.

–

On
E_{2,2}^{\omega_{1}}
there holds
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))}{4}
.

–

On
E_{3}^{\omega_{1}}
we have
r_{\mathsf{y}}(q_{\omega_{1}})\geq\frac{r_{\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}(\overline{p}))}{4}
and
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{3}^{\omega_{1}})\leq\frac{C_{3}}{N}
.

Set
E_{\omega_{2}}=E_{1,1}^{\omega_{2}}
,
E_{\omega_{2}}^{c}=E_{2}^{\omega_{2}}\cup E_{3}^{\omega_{2}}
,
E_{\omega_{1}}=E_{1,1}^{\omega_{1}}
,
E_{\omega_{1}}^{c}=E_{1,2}^{\omega_{1}}\cup E_{2,1}^{\omega_{1}}\cup E_{2,2}^{\omega_{1}}\cup E_{3}^{\omega_{1}}
, and note further that
r_{\mathsf{y}}\circ\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{z}}=r_{\mathsf{y}}
. Then,

(10.202)

\displaystyle r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1+M^{2}N}{1+N}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})+\sqrt{\frac{1+M^{2}N}{(1+N)(1+4N^{2})}}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}^{c}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}}+\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}^{c}})r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})

(10.203)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1+4N^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}^{c}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}^{c}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p}).

Moreover, we have that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{1}}(E_{\omega_{1}}^{c})\lesssim_{M}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega_{2}}(E_{\omega_{2}}^{c})\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Additionally,
\frac{1+M^{2}N}{1+N}\geq\frac{M^{2}}{4}
so that, for

(10.204)

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+4N^{2}}}\frac{M}{2},\frac{1}{1+4N^{2}}\right\},

we have

(10.205)

\displaystyle r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))-\eta r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{M^{2}}{4}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})+\frac{M}{4}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+4N^{2})}}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}^{c}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}}+\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}^{c}})r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})

(10.206)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2+8N^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{E_{\omega_{1}}^{c}\cap E_{\omega_{2}}^{c}}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p}).

Together with

(10.207)

r_{{\mathsf{y}}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}^{\kappa}))\geq r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))-|r_{{\mathsf{y}}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}^{\kappa}))-r_{{\mathsf{y}}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))|\geq r_{\mathsf{y}}(f_{N}^{3}(p_{2}))-\eta r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p}),

on
G
we obtain

(10.208)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{2}^{\kappa})|G]\leq\left(2\left(\frac{4}{M^{2}}\right)^{\alpha}+2C_{M}\frac{4^{\alpha}\left(1+4N^{2}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{M^{\alpha}\sqrt{N}}+2C_{M}\frac{(2+8N^{2})^{\alpha}}{N}\right)V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{0}).

Setting
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
and
M=4\left(\frac{8}{3}\right)^{8}
we have
2\left(\frac{4}{M^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{8}}=\frac{3}{4}
and thus we can find
N
large enough so that

(10.209)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{2}^{\kappa})|G]\leq\gamma V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{0})

for some
\gamma\in(\frac{3}{4},1)
. Finally, since

(10.210)

{\mathbb{P}}(G^{c})\leq\frac{C_{N}}{\eta^{2}r_{\mathsf{y}}(\overline{p})^{2}}\kappa

and
V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}\leq\kappa^{-\alpha/2}
, we observe that

(10.211)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\left[V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{2}^{\kappa})|G^{c}\right]{\mathbb{P}}(G^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{N}}{\eta^{2}A^{2-\alpha}}V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{0})\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}(p_{0})

for
A
large enough, the proposition is proved.
∎

10.3.5.
Local and global Lyapunov estimates

We can now combine the stochastic and inviscid regimes, exactly as in Proposition
10.14
to obtain the following local drift estimate.

Proposition 10.40
.

There exist
N>1
,
s_{0}>0
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(10.212)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)

for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

The proof of the Lyapunov-Foster drift condition concludes the section’s argument.

Proof of Proposition
10.27
.

Combining Proposition
10.40
with Lemma
10.29
, according to whether
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})
or not, yields

(10.213)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega,{\mathbf{B}}}\bigl[V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\bigr]\leq\gamma\,V_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)+\beta_{\mathsf{y}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Q}_{\mathsf{y}}(s_{0})^{c}}(p),

for all
p\in\mathsf{X}
and all sufficiently small
\kappa
, for some
\beta_{\mathsf{y}}\geq 1
.
∎

11.
Drift function for the stochastic two-point process

With the local drift
W
for the two-point process and
V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}
,
V_{{\mathsf{y}},\kappa}
and
V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}
for the one-point process near the
F_{\mathsf{x}}
,
F_{\mathsf{y}}
and
F_{\mathsf{z}}
fixed points respectively, we are now in position to write a drift function
\mathrm{V}^{(2)}
that satisfies the Lyapunov-drift condition with uniform-in-
\kappa
constants
\gamma>0
and
\beta\geq 1
.

We first define a drift function
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}
for the stochastic one-point process
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
as follows.
Recall
\chi:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,1]
is a smooth non-negative bump function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\in[0,s_{0}]
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\in[2s_{0},+\infty)
.
Recall further that for
p=(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, we have
r_{\mathsf{x}}(p)=\max\{|y|,|z|\}
,
r_{\mathsf{y}}(p)=\sqrt{x^{2}+z^{2}}
and
r_{\mathsf{z}}(p)=\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}
and also that the sets
\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s)=\{p\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}:0<r_{\sigma}(p)<s\}
, with
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{y},{\mathsf{z}}\}=\Sigma
, denote the spherical caps centred at the six fixed points.
For
s>0
small enough, the
\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s)
are mutually disjoint. We then define

(11.1)

\begin{split}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)&:=\chi(r_{\mathsf{x}})(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{y}}))(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{z}}))V_{{\mathsf{x}},\kappa}(p)+\chi(r_{\mathsf{y}})(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{z}})){V}_{\mathsf{y},\kappa}(p)+\chi(r_{\mathsf{z}})V_{{\mathsf{z}},\kappa}(p)\\
&\quad+\hat{c}(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{x}}))(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{y}}))(1-\chi(r_{\mathsf{z}}))\end{split}

where we choose
\hat{c}>0
so that
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}\geq 1
.
We further recall that there exists
0<r_{0}<s_{0}
such that, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, it holds that
f_{\omega}(p)\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0}/2)
for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
K(r_{0}):=\mathsf{X}\cap_{\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{y},{\mathsf{z}}\}}\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})^{c}
. There exists
\beta>0
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,1)
and
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(11.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma^{\prime}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)+\beta\mathbf{1}_{K(r_{0})}

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
and all
p\in\mathsf{X}
.

Proof.

Let
p\in K(r_{0})^{c}
. Then, there exists
\sigma\in\Sigma
such that
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})
and thus
f_{\omega}(p)\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0}/2)
. On

(11.3)

E=\{|r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))-r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p))|\leq\frac{s_{0}}{4}\}

there holds
r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\mathbf{1}_{E}\leq\frac{3s_{0}}{4}
and thus
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p)\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0})
. As a result,

(11.4)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\mathbf{1}_{E}]={\mathbb{E}}[V_{\sigma,\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma V_{\sigma,\kappa}(p)=\gamma\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)

in view of Propositions
10.2
,
10.15
and
10.27
. On the other hand, we observe that

(11.5)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq\frac{4}{s_{0}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))-r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p))|\right]\leq\frac{4C_{\sigma,N}}{s_{0}}\sqrt{\kappa}

thanks to Lemma
10.1
and the Lipschitz continuity of
r_{\sigma}
, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
. Together with the trivial bound
V_{\sigma,\kappa}(q)\leq(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}
we obtain

(11.6)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))|E^{c}\right]{{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})}\leq(3(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}+\hat{c})\frac{4C_{\sigma,N}}{s_{0}}\sqrt{\kappa}\leq 4C_{\sigma,N}(3(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}+\hat{c})\frac{r_{0}^{\alpha}}{s_{0}}\sqrt{\kappa}V_{\sigma,\kappa}(p)\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}V_{\sigma,\kappa}(p)

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
for
\kappa_{0}
sufficiently small. Here we have used that
r_{0}^{-\alpha}\leq V_{\sigma,\kappa}(p)
for all
r_{0}>\sqrt{\kappa}
and all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})
. As a result,

(11.7)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\gamma^{\prime}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)

for
\gamma^{\prime}=\frac{1+\gamma}{2}
, for all
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})
, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
.

Next, for
p\in K(r_{0})
, we have that
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})^{c}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
so that

(11.8)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\hat{c}+\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}{\mathbb{E}}\left[V_{\sigma,\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))\right]\leq\beta

for some
\beta>0
independent of
\kappa\in(0,1)
due to Lemma
10.3
,
10.16
and
10.29
. With this, the lemma is proved.
∎

Define next

(11.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)=\max\{\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q),\nu\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p),\nu\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(q)\},

with

(11.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{x}}(p,q)))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{y}}(p,q)))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{z}}(p,q)))W(p,q)

(11.11)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{x}}(p,q))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{y}}(p,q)))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{z}}(p,q)))W_{m,{\mathsf{x}}}(p,q)

(11.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{y}}(p,q))(1-\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{z}}(p,q)))W_{m,{\mathsf{y}}}(p,q)

(11.13)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi(\tilde{r}_{\mathsf{z}}(p,q))W_{m,{\mathsf{z}}}(p,q),

where
W_{m,\sigma}(p,q)=\max\{{W_{\sigma}(p,q),W(p,q)\}}
for
\sigma\in\Sigma
. We next show that
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}
satisfies a Lyapunov-drift condition.

Proposition 11.2
.

There exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta>0
,
s_{0}>0
,
r_{0}\leq s_{0}/2
,
\kappa_{0}>0
,
\nu>0
and
\varepsilon>0
such that for the compact set

(11.14)

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}=\{(p,q)\in\mathsf{X}^{2}:p,q\in K(\varepsilon)\text{ and }\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\geq r_{0},\text{ for all }\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}\}

there holds

(11.15)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\right]\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)+\beta\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{K}}(p,q),

for all
(p,q)\in\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
and all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
.

Proof.

Fix
0<s_{0}<s_{*}
sufficiently small, let
r_{0}<\frac{s_{0}}{2}
small enough such that
f_{\omega}(p)\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(s_{0}/2)
whenever
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(r_{0})
and
\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))<s_{0}
whenever
\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)<r_{0}
, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
. Moreover, let
\eta\in(0,r_{0})
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\eta)
. We argue as in Proposition
8.5
. First, for
(p,q)\not\in\widetilde{K}
we consider three cases.

\diamond

Case 1.
Assume first that
p,q\in K^{c}(\eta)
, so that
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma_{p}}(\eta)
and
q\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma_{q}}(\eta)
.

\diamond

Case 1.1.
Assume also that
\sigma_{p}\neq\sigma_{q}
. Hence, for
\eta
small enough we have
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\geq 1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
. Recall also that for
\eta
smaller, if necessary and in terms of
N
, we also have
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))\geq\frac{1}{2}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
. Consider next

(11.16)

\displaystyle E=\bigcap_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}}\left\{|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\leq\frac{1}{4}\right\}.

Hence, we have that
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\geq\frac{1}{4}\geq 2s_{0}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
and thus
\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))=\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)=0
. As a result, we have

(11.17)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}\right]\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

arguing as in the proof of Proposition
8.5
. On the other hand, for
E^{c}
we use Lemma
9.3
to reach

(11.18)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]\leq C{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})^{1-\xi}\leq\kappa^{\frac{1-\xi}{2}}C_{N}^{-1}C\nu^{-1}\eta^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

since
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\geq 1
and
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)\geq C_{N}\eta^{-\alpha}
, for some
C_{N}>0
, and noting that

(11.19)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\right]

(11.20)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}(p))+\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q),f_{\omega}(q))\right]

(11.21)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\kappa}

due to Lemma
10.1
. The same bound holds for
{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]
and we conclude that

(11.22)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

for
\kappa_{0}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{2}\frac{C_{N}\nu}{C\eta^{\alpha}}
small enough.

\diamond

Case 1.2.
Suppose now that
\sigma_{p}=\sigma_{q}
. As before, for
\eta<r_{0}
small enough we have either
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\geq 1
or
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}\leq r_{0}
.

\diamond

Case 1.2.1.
If
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\leq r_{0}
, then
\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{\sigma}}(p,q)\geq 1
for
\widetilde{\sigma}\neq\sigma,\mathsf{D}
and for
\eta\leq r_{0}/2
we have
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\leq r_{0}+2\eta\leq s_{0}
for
r_{0}\leq s_{0}/2
. Hence, we now consider

(11.23)

\displaystyle E=\left\{|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\leq\frac{\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\right\}.

On
E
, there holds

(11.24)

\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\leq\frac{3\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\leq r_{0},

for
\eta>0
small enough and all
p,q\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(\eta)
. In particular,
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\geq\frac{1}{2}
for
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma,\mathsf{D}
and thus

(11.25)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))=W_{m,\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))

hence we obtain

(11.26)

{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathrm{W}}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E]\leq\gamma W_{m}(p,q)

due to Proposition
9.8
for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
, for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
. On the other hand, we note from Markov inequality and Lemma
9.5
that
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq C\left(\sqrt{\kappa}+\widetilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\right)
. Recall from Lemma
9.3
that

(11.27)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq\frac{C}{\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)^{\xi}}

for all
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
, for some
\xi\in(0,1)
. Moreover, since
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)\geq 1
, we have that
{\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq C
for
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma,\mathsf{D}
. Then,

(11.28)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E^{c}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma,\mathsf{D}}{\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E^{c}]+\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma,\mathsf{D}}{\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E^{c}]

(11.29)

\displaystyle\leq\left(C+2C\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)\right){\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})^{1-\xi}

(11.30)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q){\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})^{1-\xi}

for some universal
\widetilde{C}>0
. Since
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\leq 2r_{0}
we conclude that

(11.31)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)

after choosing
r_{0}
small enough and then
\kappa_{0}
small enough. As a result, for
\gamma^{\prime}=\frac{1+\gamma}{2}
, we obtain

(11.32)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq\gamma^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{m}(p,q).

\diamond

Case 1.2.2.
We now have
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\geq 1
and
p,q\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(\eta)
. Hence, due to the symmetries of the sphere and of the rotations
\mathsf{R}_{\sigma}
, there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma\setminus\{\sigma\}
such that
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)\leq r_{0}/2
and thus
\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)=W_{m,\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)
. In particular, for
\eta>0
small enough, we have
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))\leq r_{0}
and

(11.33)

\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))=W_{m,\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))

for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
. Consider now

(11.34)

\displaystyle E=\left\{|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\leq\frac{\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\right\}

and note that on
E
there holds
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\leq 2r_{0}\leq s_{0}
. As a result,

(11.35)

\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))=W_{m,\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))

so that

(11.36)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q).

On the other hand, for
E^{c}
we argue as in Case 1.2.1 to obtain that

(11.37)

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}\right]\leq\widetilde{C}W_{m}(p,q){\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})^{1-\xi}

and

(11.38)

{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq C\left(\sqrt{\kappa}+\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)\right)\leq C(\sqrt{\kappa}+r_{0})

so that for
r_{0}>0
and
\kappa_{0}>0
small enough, we conclude first that

(11.39)

{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathrm{W}}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\right]\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}{\mathrm{W}}_{m}(p,q)

and then

(11.40)

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\right]\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

\diamond

Case 2.
Consider next
p\in K^{c}(\eta)
and
q\in K(\eta)
, so that
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma_{p}}(\eta)
and
q\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}(\eta)^{c}
, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
. We distinguish two more cases.

\diamond

Case 2.1.
Assume there exists
\tilde{\sigma}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
such that
\tilde{r}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(p,q)<r_{0}
. Then, for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
it holds
\tilde{r}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))\leq s_{0}/2
, and there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
such that
\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))=W_{\sigma^{\prime},m}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))
for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
. Consider thus

(11.41)

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\{|f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q)-f_{\omega}(q)|\leq\delta_{0}\},

(11.42)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\leq\frac{\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\right\}

and
E:=E_{1}\cap E_{2}
. Here
\delta_{0}
is such that
|r_{\sigma}(p_{1})-r_{\sigma}(p_{2})|\leq\frac{r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(q))}{2}
for all
p_{1},p_{2}\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
such that
|p_{1}-p_{2}|\leq\delta_{0}
due to the uniform continuity of
r_{\sigma}
, for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
. Note that

(11.43)

\min_{q\in K(\eta),\,\sigma\in\Sigma,\,\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}}r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(q))>0.

Then, on
E
, for
p_{1}=f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q)
and
p_{2}=f_{\omega}(q)
we have

(11.44)

r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\geq\frac{r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\geq C_{N}^{-1}\eta

due to Lemma
9.1
. Hence,

(11.45)

\displaystyle\max_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\{\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}(q)):q\in\mathsf{Q}^{c}_{\sigma}(\eta),\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}\}\leq C_{N}\eta^{-\alpha}

for
\eta
small enough. Therefore, fixing
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}
,

(11.46)

{\mathbb{E}}[\nu\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\nu C_{N}\eta^{-\frac{1}{8}}\leq\gamma\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)

once we set
\nu=\frac{1}{4}\frac{\gamma\eta^{\frac{1}{8}}\min\psi}{r_{0}^{\xi}C_{N}}
.

Next, to estimate the drift associated to the two-point chain, on
E
we also have
\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\leq 3s_{0}/4\leq s_{0}
, so that

(11.47)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]={\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime},m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]

and

(11.48)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q).

Moreover, note that

(11.49)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))^{-\xi}]\leq C_{\xi}\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)^{-\xi}

due to Lemma
9.3
. If
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\geq 4r_{0}\left(\frac{C_{\xi}\max\psi}{\min\psi}\right)^{\frac{1}{\xi}}
, we have that

(11.50)

\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)\leq\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)^{-\xi}\max\psi\leq\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)^{-\xi}\min\psi\leq\mathrm{W}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q),

so that
\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)=W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)
. Hence,

(11.51)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[W(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq C_{\xi}\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)^{-\xi}\max\psi\leq 4^{-\xi}W_{\sigma^{\prime}}(p,q)\leq\gamma^{\prime}\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q),

for
\gamma^{\prime}=\max\{\gamma,4^{-\xi}\}\in(0,1)
. On the other hand, if
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\leq 4r_{0}\left(\frac{C_{\xi}\max\psi}{\min\psi}\right)^{\frac{1}{\xi}}
, we choose
r_{0}
small enough so that
\widetilde{r}_{\mathsf{D}}(p,q)\leq s_{*}
from Proposition
9.8
. Hence,

(11.52)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[W(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma W(p,q)

and we conclude that

(11.53)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma^{\prime}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

For the complementary event we write

(11.54)

\displaystyle E^{c}=E_{1}^{c}\cup E_{2}^{c}=(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})\cup(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c})

Note that from Markov inequality and Lemmas
10.1
and
9.5
that

(11.55)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c})\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa},\quad{\mathbb{P}}(E_{2}^{c})\lesssim(\sqrt{\kappa}+r_{0}).

We argue for each subset.

–

For
E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}
we have
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2})\leq{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}
. We now have

(11.56)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}]\leq(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}\leq\left(\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)^{\alpha}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(q)

due to the pointwise bound
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\leq(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}
. Therefore,

(11.57)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}}]\leq\sqrt{\kappa}\left(\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

–

For
E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}
we have
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}
again. We now observe that

(11.58)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}}]\leq C\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q){\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})^{1-\xi}

due to Lemma
9.3
and we still have
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\leq\left(\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)^{\alpha}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(q)
.
Hence,

(11.59)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}}]\leq\left(\left(\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)^{\alpha}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})^{1-\xi}\right)\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

as well for
\kappa_{0}
small enough.

–

For
E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}
we have
{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}+r_{0}
.
We still have

(11.60)

{\mathbb{E}}[\nu\mathrm{V}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq\nu C_{N}\eta^{-\frac{1}{8}}\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

and also

(11.61)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}}]\leq C\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q){\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c})^{1-\xi}

due to Lemma
9.3
. As a result,

(11.62)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}}]\leq(\gamma+C{\mathbb{P}}(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c})^{1-\xi})\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

With this, we conclude that

(11.63)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}}]

(11.64)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}}]

(11.65)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\gamma^{\prime}}{2}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

after choosing
r_{0}
and
\kappa_{0}
small enough, once we note that
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}
can be made arbitrarily small because
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}<1
.

\diamond

Case 2.2 .
On the other hand, if
\tilde{r}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(p,q)\geq r_{0}
for all
\tilde{\sigma}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
and
(p,q)\not\in\widetilde{K}
we must have
p\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma_{p}}(\varepsilon)
, for some
\sigma_{p}\in\Sigma
as
q\in K(\eta)\subset K(\varepsilon)
. We begin by noting that from Lemma
9.3
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq Cr_{0}^{-\xi}
. Consider again

(11.66)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\{|f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q)-f_{\omega}(q)|\leq\delta_{0}\},

so that on
E
there holds
r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\geq\frac{r_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\geq C_{N}^{-1}\eta
due to Lemma
9.1
.
Hence,

(11.67)

{\mathbb{E}}[\nu\mathrm{V}(f_{\omega}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\nu C_{N}\eta^{-\frac{1}{8}}\leq Cr_{0}^{-\xi}

due to the choice of
\nu=\frac{\gamma\eta^{\alpha}\min\psi}{4C_{N}r_{0}^{\xi}}
. Since
\varepsilon^{-\alpha}\leq\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)
for
\kappa_{0}<\varepsilon^{2}
small enough, we have that

(11.68)

\displaystyle Cr_{0}^{-\xi}\leq\nu\gamma\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)

provided that
Cr_{0}^{-\xi}\leq\nu\gamma\varepsilon^{-\alpha}
, that is,
\varepsilon\leq\left(\frac{\nu\gamma r_{0}^{\xi}}{C}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}
. Hence, for
\varepsilon>0
small enough and
\kappa_{0}<\varepsilon^{2}
smaller, we find that

(11.69)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\nu\gamma\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

On the contrary, on
E^{c}
we have
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}
and

(11.70)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|E^{c}]{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq\sqrt{\kappa}(\sqrt{\kappa})^{-\alpha}\leq\sqrt{\kappa}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)^{\alpha}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(q),

while we still have

(11.71)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]\leq Cr_{0}^{-\xi}{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})^{1-\xi}\leq\nu\gamma\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)

for the same choice of
\varepsilon
. As a result,

(11.72)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]\leq\left({\kappa}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+\gamma\kappa^{\frac{1-\xi}{2}}\right)\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

With this,

(11.73)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}]

(11.74)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

for
\kappa_{0}
small enough since
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}<1
.

\diamond

Case 3.
Assume finally that
p,q\in K(\eta)\subset K(\varepsilon)
. Hence,
p,q\in\mathsf{Q}_{\sigma}^{c}(\eta)
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
and we must then have
\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)<r_{0}
, for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
. Therefore,
\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))<s_{0}/2
for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
and there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{D}}
such that
\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))=W_{\sigma^{\prime},m}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))
, for all
\omega\in[-N,N]^{2}
. In particular,
\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q)=W_{\sigma^{\prime},m}(p,q)
. We consider here

(11.75)

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\{|f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p)-f_{\omega}(p)|\leq\delta_{0}\},

(11.76)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\{|f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q)-f_{\omega}(q)|\leq\delta_{0}\},

(11.77)

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{|\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))-\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))|\leq\frac{\widetilde{r}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(f_{\omega}(p),f_{\omega}(q))}{2}\right\}

where
\delta_{0}
is given as before. Then, on
E=E_{1}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3}
we have
\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))=W_{\sigma^{\prime},m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))
, which yields

(11.78)

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{W}_{m}}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}\right]\leq\gamma\mathrm{W}_{m}(p,q).

Additionally, we recall that

(11.79)

\max\{\nu\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p)),\nu\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\}\mathbf{1}_{E}\leq\nu C_{N}\eta^{-\alpha}\leq\gamma W(p,q).

As a result, there holds

(11.80)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{E}]\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q).

For the complement
E^{c}
, we now write
E^{c}=E_{1}^{c}\cup E_{2}^{c}\cup E_{3}^{c}
where

(11.81)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{c}=(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3}^{c})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3}^{c})

as well as

(11.82)

\displaystyle E_{2}^{c}=(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3})\cup(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3}^{c})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3}^{c})

and

(11.83)

\displaystyle E_{3}^{c}=(E_{1}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3}^{c})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3}^{c})\cup(E_{1}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3}^{c})\cup(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c}\cap E_{3}^{c})

We argue for each of these sets
\widetilde{E}
as in Case 2.1 above, noting that either

(11.84)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))|\widetilde{E}]\leq\kappa^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

with
{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{E})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}
or

(11.85)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{E}}]\leq C\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q){\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{E})^{1-\xi}

with
{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{E})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}+r_{0}
. Since
\alpha=\frac{1}{8}<1
, choosing
r_{0}
and
\kappa_{0}
small enough we conclude that

(11.86)

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))\right]\leq\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)

as well.

End of proof.
Finally, for
(p,q)\in\widetilde{K}
, we have that
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{W}_{m}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq Cr_{0}^{-\xi}
due to Lemma
9.3
. Moreover, we have that

(11.87)

{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p))]\leq\gamma^{\prime}\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(p)+\beta\leq\widetilde{\beta}

for some
\widetilde{\beta}>0
for
\kappa_{0}
small enough and similarly for
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]
. With this, we conclude that

(11.88)

{\mathbb{E}}[\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(p),f_{\omega}^{\kappa}(q))]\leq\beta^{\prime},

for some universal
\beta^{\prime}>0
for all
(p,q)\in\widetilde{K}
.
∎

12.
Uniform-in-
\kappa
exponential mixing and enhanced dissipation

In this section we culminate the analysis on the stochastic flow map
f_{\omega}^{\kappa}
and we show that the stochastic Markov chain
P_{\kappa}^{(2)}
satisfies the hypothesis
(H_{\kappa})
introduced in Section
2
, namely there exists
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta>0
,
R>\frac{2\beta}{1-\gamma}
,
\upsilon>0
and a probability measure
\nu_{0}
such that for all
\kappa>0
sufficiently small there holds

(12.1)

\displaystyle P^{(2),\ell}_{\kappa}\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{\kappa}\leq\gamma\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{\kappa}+\beta,

and

(12.2)

\displaystyle\inf_{(p,q)\in\{\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{\kappa}\leq R\}}P^{(2),\ell}_{\kappa}((p,q),\cdot)\geq\upsilon\nu_{0}(\cdot),

for some
\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{\kappa}:\mathsf{X}^{(2)}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
.

Note that (
12.1
) is satisfied for the drift function
\mathrm{V}_{\kappa}^{(2)}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}
thanks to Proposition
11.2
.
Hence, this section is devoted to prove the small set condition (
12.2
).
To do so, we follow closely the approach used in
[
10
]
, expanding and adapting the discussion to our sphere setting, where necessary.
More precisely, we start with Lemma
12.1
(a quantitative version of
[
10
]
*Lemma 5.2) which is then used in Lemma
12.2
to establish the existence of an open small set for the stochastic two-point process (see
[
10
]
*Lemma 5.1 for reference).

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
f:B_{r}(0)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be a
C^{1}
map such that
f(0)=0
and
\mathrm{rank}(Df(0))=d
. Then there exist constants
\delta,s,C>0
such that, for every Lipschitz map
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
satisfying
\left\lVert f-g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(0))}\leq\delta
, one has

(1)

B_{s}(0)\subset g(B_{r}(0))
, and,

(2)

for every Borel set
A\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

(12.3)

|g^{-1}(A)\cap B_{r}(0)|\geq C\,\mathrm{Lip}(g)^{-d}|A\cap B_{s}(0)|.

Proof.

The inclusion
B_{s}(0)\subset g(B_{r}(0))
is precisely Lemma 5.2 of
[
10
]
, after choosing
\delta,s>0
sufficiently small. We prove only the quantitative estimate.

By the constant-rank theorem, there exists open sets
\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and diffeomorphisms

(12.4)

\displaystyle\psi:B^{d}_{\rho}\times B^{N-d}_{R}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\rightarrow\psi(B_{\rho}^{d}\times B_{R}^{N-d})\subset B_{r}(0)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N},

(12.5)

\displaystyle\varphi:\mathcal{U}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

for
\rho,R>0
small enough, such that for all
(u,v)\in B^{d}_{\rho}\times B_{R}^{N-d}
there holds
(\varphi\circ f\circ\psi)(u,v)=u
. Here we denote by
B^{d}_{\rho}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
B^{N-d}_{R}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N-d}
the balls of radius
\rho
and
R
centred at the origin of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{N-d}
, respectively.

For
\delta>0
sufficiently small,
g\circ\psi
remains in
\mathcal{U}
, and the slice maps

(12.6)

h_{v}:B_{\rho}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\qquad h_{v}(u):=(\varphi\circ g\circ\psi)(u,v),

satisfy the covering property

(12.7)

B_{\sigma}^{d}\subset h_{v}(B_{\rho}^{d}),

for every
v\in B_{R}^{N-d}
and for some
0<\sigma<\rho
. This is the degree argument contained in the proof of
[
10
]
*Lemma 5.2.
We choose
s>0
so small that

(12.8)

A_{s}:=A\cap B_{s}(0)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathcal{W}:=\varphi(A_{s})\subset B_{\sigma}^{d}.

Since the diffeomorphisms
\varphi
and
\psi
are fixed on pre-compact sets, there exists
C_{1}>0
such that for every
v\in B_{R}^{N-d}
it holds
\mathrm{Lip}(h_{v})\leq C_{1}\mathrm{Lip}(g)
.
Moreover, after decreasing
s
if necessary, the change of variables under
\varphi
gives

(12.9)

|\mathcal{W}|=|\varphi(A_{s})|\geq C_{2}|A_{s}|

for some
C_{2}>0
.
For
v\in B_{R}^{N-d}
, define
E_{v}:=\{u\in B_{\rho}^{d}:h_{v}(u)\in\mathcal{W}\}
.
By (
12.7
), the multiplicity function
N(h_{v},E_{v},y):=\mathcal{H}^{0}(E_{v}\cap h_{v}^{-1}\{y\})
satisfies
N(h_{v},E_{v},y)\geq 1
for every
y\in\mathcal{W}
, where
\mathcal{H}^{0}
denotes the zero-dimensional Hausdorff measure. The area formula on the
d
-dimensional slices
[
23
]
*Lemma 5.2 gives

(12.10)

|\mathcal{W}|=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{W}}\mathrm{d}y\leq C_{d}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{W}}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)\leq C_{d}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}N(h_{v},E_{v},y)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)=C_{d}\int_{E_{v}}|Jh_{v}(u)|\mathrm{d}u,

where
\mathcal{H}^{d}
denotes the
d
-dimensional Hausdorff measure,
C_{d}>0
and
Jh_{v}
is the Jacobian determinant of
h_{v}
.
Since
h_{v}
is Lipschitz, for a.e.
u\in B_{\rho}^{d}

(12.11)

|Jh_{v}(u)|\leq\mathrm{Lip}(h_{v})^{d}\leq C_{1}^{d}\,\mathrm{Lip}(g)^{d}.

Therefore, for every
v\in B_{R}^{N-d}
, there holds

(12.12)

|E_{v}|\geq C_{1}^{-d}C_{d}^{-1}\,\mathrm{Lip}(g)^{-d}|\mathcal{W}|,

and by Fubini,

(12.13)

\displaystyle\left|\left\{(u,v)\in B_{\rho}^{d}\times B_{R}^{N-d}:(\varphi\circ g\circ\psi)(u,v)\in\mathcal{W}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{R}^{N-d}}|E_{v}|\,{\rm d}v\geq|B_{R}^{N-d}|\,C_{1}^{-d}\,\mathrm{Lip}(g)^{-d}|\mathcal{W}|.

Moreover,

(12.14)

\displaystyle\psi\left(\left\{(u,v)\in B_{\rho}^{d}\times B_{R}^{N-d}:(\varphi\circ g\circ\psi)(u,v)\in\mathcal{W}\right\}\right)=g^{-1}(A_{s})\cap\psi(B^{d}_{\rho}\times B^{N-d}_{R})\subseteq g^{-1}(A)\cap B_{r}(0)

since
A_{s}\subseteq A
and
\psi(B^{d}_{\rho}\times B^{N-d}_{R})\subset B_{r}(0)
, and thus

(12.15)

\displaystyle\left|\left\{(u,v)\in B_{\rho}^{d}\times B_{R}^{N-d}:(\varphi\circ g\circ\psi)(u,v)\in\mathcal{W}\right\}\right|\leq C_{\psi}^{-1}\left|g^{-1}(A)\cap B_{r}(0)\right|.

for some
C_{\psi}:=\inf_{B_{R}^{d}\times B_{\rho}^{N-d}}|\det D\psi|>0
since
\psi
is a diffeomorphism on
B^{d}_{\rho}\times B^{N-d}_{R}
to its image. Combining this with (
12.9
) yields, for some
C>0
,

(12.16)

|g^{-1}(A)\cap B_{r}(0)|\geq C\mathrm{Lip}(g)^{-d}|A\cap B_{s}(0)|

and the proof is finished.
∎

Lemma 12.2
.

There exists non-empty open sets
U_{1},U_{2}\subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
and a constant
\psi_{0}>0
such that, for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(12.17)

\displaystyle\inf_{({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in U_{1}}P_{\kappa}^{(2),n}((x,y),\cdot)\geq\beta_{0}\mathrm{Leb}|_{U_{2}}(\cdot)

for all
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
, for some
\kappa_{0}
small enough.

Proof.

We argue as in
[
10
]
*Lemma 5.1. From the proof of Proposition
8.3
there exists
({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0})\in\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
,
n\geq 1
and
\underline{\omega}_{n}
such that the map
\underline{\omega}\mapsto f_{\underline{\omega}}^{(2)}({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0})
is a submersion at
\underline{\omega}=\underline{\omega}_{n}
, with
\varrho_{n}(\underline{\omega})\geq c>0
for all
\underline{\omega}\in B_{\varepsilon}(\underline{\omega}_{n})
for some
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\eta>0
and
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in B_{\eta}(({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0}))
. Let
\delta>0
and consider the set

(12.18)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\left\{\sup_{\underline{\omega}\in[-N,N]^{2n}}|f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{x}})-f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}_{0})|+|f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}_{0})-f_{\underline{\omega}}({\mathbf{x}}_{0})|+|f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{y}})-f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}_{0})|+|f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}_{0})-f_{\underline{\omega}}({\mathbf{y}}_{0})|\leq\frac{\delta}{100}\right\}

(12.19)

\displaystyle\quad\bigcap\left\{\sup_{\underset{\omega\neq\varpi}{\underline{\omega},\underline{\varpi}\in[-N,N]^{2n}}}\frac{|f_{\underline{\omega}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})-f_{\underline{\varpi}}^{\kappa}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|}{|\underline{\omega}-\underline{\varpi}|_{\infty}}\leq M\right\}

and note that
{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}+\eta+M^{-2}
due to Markov inequality and Lemma
9.6
and Lemma
9.7
for all
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in B_{\eta}(({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0}))
. Hence,
{\mathbb{P}}(E)\geq\frac{3}{4}
for all
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in U_{1}:=B_{\eta}(({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0}))
for some
M>1
large enough, some
\eta>0
small enough and
\kappa_{0}
small enough. Moreover, on
E
we have that
|f_{\omega}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})-f_{\omega}^{(2)}({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0})|\leq\frac{\delta}{25}
for all
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in U_{1}
and all
\underline{\omega}\in[-N,N]^{2n}
and thus apply
[
10
]
*Lemma 5.2 to the map
g:\underline{\omega}\mapsto f_{\underline{\omega}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})
to conclude that
g(B_{\varepsilon}(\underline{\omega}_{n}))\supseteq B_{s}(f_{\underline{\omega}_{n}}({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0}))=:U_{2}
, for some
s>0
. Hence, for all measurable
U\subset\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{X}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}
,

(12.20)

\displaystyle P^{(2),\kappa}_{n}(({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),U)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\left[\int_{g^{-1}(U)\cap B_{\varepsilon}(\underline{\omega}_{n})}\varrho_{n}(\underline{\omega})\mathrm{d}\underline{\omega}\big|E\right]{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{B}}(E)

(12.21)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3c}{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\left[|g^{-1}(U)\cap B_{\varepsilon}(\underline{\omega}_{n})|\big|E\right]

(12.22)

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime}|U\cap B_{s}(f_{\underline{\omega}_{n}}^{(2)}({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{0}))|

for some
c^{\prime}>0
. Here we have used that
\varrho_{n}(\underline{\omega})\geq c
for all
\underline{\omega}\in B_{\varepsilon}(\underline{\omega}_{n})
and Lemma
12.1
, since
f_{\omega}^{(2)}
is a local submersion and
g
has a uniform Lipschitz bound on the set
E
.
∎

We are now in position to verify the Lyapunov-Foster drift condition (
12.1
) and prove small set condition (
12.2
) in
(H_{\kappa})
.
To do so, we follow the two step proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
10
]
, which we split into three auxiliary lemmas:

–

Lemma
12.3
proves that every compact set can be uniformly reached via the inviscid two-point process;

–

Lemma
12.4
then translate the above inviscid property into a uniform in
\kappa>0
small set condition for all compact sets for the stochastic two-point process, combining Lemma
12.3
with Lemma
12.2
;

–

Lemma
12.5
shows that level sets of
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}
are contained in compact sets.

Once these result are established, Proposition
12.6
proves that
(H_{\kappa})
holds.

Lemma 12.3
.

For every compact set
K\Subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
and every non-empty open set
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
, there exist
q_{K}\in\mathbb{N}
and
c_{K}>0
such that

(12.23)

\inf_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K}P_{0}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},\mathcal{O})\geq c_{K}.

Proof.

The continuity of the inviscid two-point RDS gives the Feller property of
P_{0}^{(2)}
, Lemma
2.3
gives aperiodicity, Lemma
8.3
gives the open-small-set structure, and Proposition
8.4
gives topological irreducibility on
\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
. Combined with Theorem 6.2.5 (ii) and Theorem 5.5.7 in
[
36
]
we obtain (
12.23
) (see
[
10
]
).
∎

Lemma 12.4
.

Let
K\Subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
be compact. There exists an integer
\ell_{K}\in\mathbb{N}
, a constant
\upsilon_{K}>0
, a probability measure
\nu_{0}
on
\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
, and
\kappa_{K}>0
such that, for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{K})
,

(12.24)

\inf_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K}P_{\kappa}^{(2),\ell_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},\cdot)\geq\upsilon_{K}\nu_{0}(\cdot).

Proof.

By Lemma
12.2
, there exist non-empty open sets
U_{1},U_{2}\Subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
, an integer
n_{\ast}\in\mathbb{N}
, a constant
\beta_{\ast}>0
, and
\kappa_{\ast}>0
such that

(12.25)

\inf_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in U_{1}}P_{\kappa}^{(2),n_{\ast}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},\cdot)\geq\beta_{\ast}\,\mathrm{Leb}(\cdot\cap U_{2})

for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{\ast})
. Choose
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)}\in U_{1}
and
r>0
such that
B_{r}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\Subset U_{1}
. Applying Lemma
12.3
with
\mathcal{O}:=B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})
, we obtain
q_{K}\in\mathbb{N}
and
c_{K}>0
such that

(12.26)

\inf_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K}P_{0}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)\geq c_{K}.

We now extend this deterministic lower bound to the stochastic chain.

For each
{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K
the measure
P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},\cdot)
converges weakly-* to the measure
P_{0}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},\cdot)
as
\kappa\rightarrow 0
so that there exists
\kappa_{0}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})>0
such that

(12.27)

\displaystyle\inf_{\kappa<\kappa_{0}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})}P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)\geq\frac{1}{2}P_{0}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)\geq\frac{c_{K}}{2}.

This pointwise lower bound is stable under small perturbations of the initial condition. Fix
{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K
and define, for
{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)}
close to
{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}
,

(12.28)

E:=\left\{\sup_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}\in[-N,N]^{2q_{K}}}d_{\mathsf{X}^{(2)}}\left(f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}),f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)})\right)\leq\frac{r}{2}\right\}.

By the uniform-in-
\underline{\omega}
stability estimate in Lemma
9.6
, there exists
\widetilde{C}=\widetilde{C}(q_{K},N)>0
such that

(12.29)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\left[\sup_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}\in[-N,N]^{2q_{K}}}d_{\mathsf{X}^{(2)}}\left(f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}),f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)})\right)\right]\leq\widetilde{C}\,d_{\mathsf{X}^{(2)}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)}).

Choose
r_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}}:=\frac{c_{K}r}{8\widetilde{C}}
, decreasing it if necessary so that
B_{r_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})\Subset X^{(2)}
. Then, for every
{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)}\in B_{r_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})
, Markov’s inequality gives

(12.30)

{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})\leq\frac{2}{r}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{B}}\left[\sup_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}\in[-N,N]^{2q_{K}}}d_{\mathsf{X}^{(2)}}\left(f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}),f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)})\right)\right]\leq\frac{c_{K}}{4}.

On
E
one has

(12.31)

\left\{f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})\in B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\right\}\subset\left\{f_{\underline{\omega}_{q_{K}}}^{(2),\kappa}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)})\in B_{r}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\right\}.

Combining this inclusion with (
12.27
), we obtain

(12.32)

\displaystyle P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)},B_{r}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},B_{r/2}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}(E^{c})

(12.33)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{K}}{2}-\frac{c_{K}}{4}=\frac{c_{K}}{4}.

for
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}))
. The balls
B_{r_{{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)})
, with
{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K
, form an open cover of
K
.
By compactness, choose a finite subcover
K\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{J}B_{r_{{\mathbf{x}}_{j}^{(2)}}}({\mathbf{x}}_{j}^{(2)})
, and let
\kappa_{K}^{\prime}:=\min_{1\leq j\leq J}\kappa_{{\mathbf{x}}_{j}^{(2)}}
.
Then, for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{K}^{\prime})
,

(12.34)

\inf_{{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)}\in K}P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}\bigl({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)},B_{r}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\bigr)\geq\frac{c_{K}}{4}.

Since
B_{r}({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{(2)})\Subset U_{1}
, this implies

(12.35)

\inf_{{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)}\in K}P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)},U_{1})\geq a_{K},\qquad a_{K}:=\frac{c_{K}}{4}.

Combining (
12.35
) with (
12.25
), the Chapman–Kolmogorov argument (see also
[
36
]
*Theorem 3.4.2) from the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
10
]
gives, for every Borel set
U\subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
and every
{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\in K
,

(12.36)

\displaystyle P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}+n_{\ast}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},U)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{U_{1}}P_{\kappa}^{(2),n_{\ast}}({\mathbf{y}}^{(2)},U)P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},{\rm d}{\mathbf{y}}^{(2)})

(12.37)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{\ast}\mathrm{Leb}(U\cap U_{2})P_{\kappa}^{(2),q_{K}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(2)},U_{1})

(12.38)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{\ast}a_{K}\mathrm{Leb}(U\cap U_{2}).

Since
U_{2}
is non-empty and open,
\mathrm{Leb}(U_{2})>0
. Define next

(12.39)

\nu_{0}(U):=\frac{\mathrm{Leb}(U\cap U_{2})}{\mathrm{Leb}(U_{2})},\qquad\upsilon_{K}:=\beta_{\ast}a_{K}\mathrm{Leb}(U_{2})>0,\qquad\ell_{K}:=q_{K}+n_{\ast}.

After decreasing
\kappa_{K}^{\prime}>0
so that
\kappa_{K}^{\prime}\leq\kappa_{\ast}
, we obtain (
12.24
) with
\kappa_{K}:=\kappa_{K}^{\prime}
.
∎

Together, the two lemmas above constitute the first step in the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
10
]
while the second step is carried out in the following lemma.

Lemma 12.5
.

For every
R>0
there exist
\kappa_{R}>0
and a compact set
\widetilde{K}_{R}\Subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
such that

(12.40)

\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq R\}\subset\widetilde{K}_{R}

for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{R})
.

Proof.

Choose parameters
0<\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq r_{0}^{\prime}\leq\frac{s_{0}}{2}
so small that

(12.41)

C(r_{0}^{\prime})^{-\xi}>2R,\qquad C(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{-1/8}>2R.

Then choose
\kappa_{R}>0
so small that
\sqrt{\kappa}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{R})
. Define

(12.42)

\widetilde{K}_{R}:=\left\{(p,q)\in\mathsf{X}^{(2)}:p,q\in K(\varepsilon^{\prime}),\quad\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)\geq r_{0}^{\prime}\text{ for every }\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathsf{D}}}\right\}.

By construction,
\widetilde{K}_{R}\Subset\mathsf{X}^{(2)}
. We prove that
(\widetilde{K}_{R})^{c}\subset\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}>R\}
. Let
(p,q)\notin\widetilde{K}_{R}
so that at least one of the following alternatives holds.

–

There exists
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathsf{D}}}
such that
\tilde{r}_{\sigma}(p,q)<r_{0}^{\prime}
. By the two-point singular part of
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}
, we get

(12.43)

\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)\geq C(r_{0}^{\prime})^{-\xi}>2R.

–

There exists
\sigma\in\Sigma
such that
r_{\sigma}(p)<\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Since
\sqrt{\kappa}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
, the one-point part of
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}
gives

(12.44)

\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)\geq C\max\{\sqrt{\kappa},r_{\sigma}(p)\}^{-1/8}\geq C(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{-1/8}>2R.

–

There exists
\sigma\in\Sigma
such that
r_{\sigma}(q)<\varepsilon^{\prime}
. The same argument gives

(12.45)

\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)\geq C\max\{\sqrt{\kappa},r_{\sigma}(q)\}^{-1/8}\geq C(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{-1/8}>2R.

In all three cases,
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}(p,q)>2R
. Hence
(\widetilde{K}_{R})^{c}\subset\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}>R\}
, which is equivalent to
\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq R\}\subset\widetilde{K}_{R}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Checking the
(H_{\kappa})
condition now follows by combining the various ingredients established so far.

Proposition 12.6
.

There exists
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta>0
,
R>\frac{2\beta}{1-\gamma}
,
\upsilon>0
, and a probability measure
\nu_{0}
such that, for all
\kappa>0
sufficiently small,

(12.46)

P^{(2),\ell}_{\kappa}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq\gamma\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}+\beta,

and

(12.47)

\inf_{({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq R\}}P^{(2),\ell}_{\kappa}(({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),\cdot)\geq\upsilon\nu_{0}(\cdot).

Proof.

Let
\gamma_{0}\in(0,1)
and
b_{0}>0
be the constants given by Proposition
11.2
, so that, after decreasing
\kappa_{0}>0
if necessary,

(12.48)

P_{\kappa}^{(2)}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq\gamma_{0}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}+b_{0}

for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
. Iterating (
12.48
), as in the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
10
]
, gives for every
m\in\mathbb{N}

(12.49)

P_{\kappa}^{(2),m}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq\gamma_{0}^{m}\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}+b_{0}\frac{1-\gamma_{0}^{m}}{1-\gamma_{0}}.

Choose

(12.50)

R>\frac{2b_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}.

By Lemma
12.5
, there exist
\kappa_{R}>0
and a compact set
\widetilde{K}_{R}\Subset X^{(2)}
such that
\{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{\kappa}\leq R\}\subset\widetilde{K}_{R}
for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{R})
. Applying Lemma
12.4
to
K=\widetilde{K}_{R}
, we obtain
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\upsilon>0
, a probability measure
\nu_{0}
, and
\kappa_{R}^{\prime}>0
such that

(12.51)

\inf_{\zeta^{(2)}\in\widetilde{K}_{R}}P_{\kappa}^{(2),\ell}(\zeta^{(2)},\cdot)\geq\upsilon\nu_{0}(\cdot)

for every
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{R}^{\prime})
. Hence (
12.47
) follows for every
\kappa\in(0,\min\{\kappa_{R},\kappa_{R}^{\prime},\kappa_{0}\})
. Finally, define

(12.52)

\gamma:=\gamma_{0}^{\ell},\qquad\beta:=b_{0}\frac{1-\gamma_{0}^{\ell}}{1-\gamma_{0}}.

Then (
12.49
) with
m=\ell
gives (
12.46
). Moreover,
\frac{2\beta}{1-\gamma}=\frac{2b_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}
, and therefore (
12.50
) gives
R>\frac{2\beta}{1-\gamma}
.
The proof is complete.
∎

We finish the section by presenting the main ideas behind the proof of Corollary
1.3
.

Proof of Corollary
1.3
.

Following the proof of Proposition 2.2 in
[
37
]
, in view of Theorem
1.1
and Corollary
1.2
, there exists a deterministic
\lambda>0
and a random constant
\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}\geq 1
independent of
{\mathbf{B}}
such that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\omega}[|\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}|^{2}]\leq\mathrm{C}^{\prime}_{2}
, for some
\mathrm{C}^{\prime}_{2}\geq 1
uniformly in
\kappa
, for which

(12.53)

\displaystyle\|\rho(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{\kappa}}e^{-\lambda t}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

Note that the trivial monotonicity estimate
\|\rho(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}
is sharper for small times
t\lesssim\log\frac{1}{\kappa}
. For the critical time

(12.54)

\displaystyle t_{*}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(1+\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)\right)

there holds
\|\rho(t_{*},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{e}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}
. Using the monotonicity of
\|\rho(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}
and iterating the above estimate we obtain

(12.55)

\displaystyle\|\rho(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{-n}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{-\mu(\kappa)t}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and all
t\in[nt_{*},(n+1)t_{*})
, where we define the random rate
\mu(\kappa)
as

(12.56)

\displaystyle\mu(\kappa):=\lambda\left(1+\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\right)\right)^{-1}.

Since
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}\geq 1
we readily obtain
\mu(\kappa)\leq 2\lambda
almost surely. Moreover, for any fixed
M>1
,

(12.57)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu(\kappa)\log(\kappa^{-1})\leq\frac{2\lambda}{1+M}\right)={\mathbb{P}}\left(|\mathrm{C}_{\underline{\omega},\kappa}^{\prime}|^{2}\geq e^{-2}\kappa^{-M}\right)\leq e^{2}\kappa^{M}\mathrm{C}^{\prime}\leq e^{2}\kappa_{0}^{M}\mathrm{C}^{\prime}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Rossby–Haurwitz flows simulation
